\frac{a(T-\theta_{1})}{T}&1+\frac{a^{2}(T-\theta_{1})}{T}\end{bmatrix},

which also always converges to some positive definite matrix. Indeed, it suffices to ensure the determinant is nonzero, and the determinant is
1+a^{2}z(1-z)
with
z=(T-\theta_{1})/T\in[0,1]
.

(c)

We now demonstrate some scenarios where the linear spacing condition is necessary. Consider

\displaystyle A_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}\text{ \ and \ }A_{2,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}.

Then, for

\displaystyle\bar{\Sigma}_{A,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\left\{\begin{bmatrix}\theta_{1}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&T-\theta_{1}\end{bmatrix}\right\}=\begin{bmatrix}\theta_{1}/T&0\\
0&1-\theta_{1}/T\end{bmatrix},

to be positive definite, it requires
\min(\theta_{1},T-\theta_{1})
to be of order
T
.

We now consider the case with
q=2
:

(d)

Let

\displaystyle A_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},\,A_{2,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\text{ \ and \ }A_{3,1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\Sigma}_{A,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\left\{\begin{bmatrix}\theta_{1}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}T-\theta_{2}&0\\
0&T-\theta_{2}\end{bmatrix}\right\}=\begin{bmatrix}1-\frac{(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{T}&0\\
0&1-\frac{\theta_{1}}{T}\end{bmatrix},

which can be positive definite even when
\theta_{1}/T=o(1)
as long as
T-\theta_{2}\asymp T
.

We conclude this subsection by presenting the following proposition which encompasses all the examples discussed previously.

Proposition A.2
.

Let Assumptions
’
‣
A.3.1
(i)–(ii) hold. Then given any index set of segments
\mathcal{Q}\subset[q+1]
such that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{Q}}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})/T=o(1)
, Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(iii) is equivalent to

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in[q+1]\setminus\mathcal{Q}}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}\to\Sigma_{A,k}^{\ast},

for some positive definite matrix
\Sigma_{A,k}^{\ast}
.

Proof of Proposition
A.2
.

Note that
\mathcal{Q}
can be empty, and the equivalence is trivial. Suppose that
\mathcal{Q}
is non-empty. Noting that
\mathcal{Q}\neq[q+1]
(otherwise it contradicts that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{Q}}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})/T=o(1)
), both directions are shown as follows.

“
\Rightarrow
”:

We show the implication using (in fact, the unique choice)
\Sigma_{A,k}^{\ast}=\Sigma_{A,k}
from Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(ii), which is direct by

\displaystyle\Sigma_{A,k}

\displaystyle=\lim_{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in[q+1]}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\lim_{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in[q+1]\setminus\mathcal{Q}}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{Q}}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\lim_{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in[q+1]\setminus\mathcal{Q}}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal},

where the last equality used
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{Q}}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})/T=o(1)
and Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(i).

“
\Leftarrow
”:

Similar to the above, we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in[q+1]}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\to\Sigma_{A,k}^{\ast}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{Q}}\big\|\otimes_{\ell=K,\,\ell\neq k}^{1}A_{j,\ell}\big\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}=:\Sigma_{A,k}^{\ast}+\Sigma_{A,k}^{\mathcal{Q}}.

Combining
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{Q}}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})/T=o(1)
and Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(i), we have
\|\Sigma_{A,k}^{\mathcal{Q}}\|=o(1)
and hence
\Sigma_{A,k}=\Sigma_{A,k}^{\ast}
by Weyl’s inequality and the definition of
\Sigma_{A,k}
from Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(ii). Thus Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(iii) is true.

The equivalence is then concluded and so is the proof of this proposition.
∎

A.3.2
Modification of TFMseg

In the absence of Assumption
3
(iv), it is required to expand the search space for the change points, to ensure that for each
\theta_{j},\,j\in[q]
, there exists
(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\in\mathbb{M}
containing
\theta_{j}
(and no other change point) whose length is sufficiently large for detecting the change point therein.
It is achieved by setting
\mu_{T}\asymp\log_{2}(T)
and
\varpi_{T}\asymp\psi_{T}^{2}
, where
\psi_{T}=\max\{T^{2/(\nu-2)},\sqrt{\log(T)}\}

for
\nu>6
, see Assumptions
1
and
4
.
Then, upon carefully inspecting the proof of Theorem
2
, provided that
\min_{j\in[q]}\omega_{j}^{2}\Delta_{j}/\psi_{T}^{2}\to\infty
, we have detection consistency (
\mathbb{P}\{\widehat{q}=q\}\to 1
) as well as

\max_{j\in[q]}\omega_{j}^{2}|\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}|=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\psi_{T}^{2}\right).

This shows that Assumption
3
(iv) enables considerably improving the theoretical performance of TFMseg, with Assumption
5
that only requires
\min_{j\in[q]}\omega_{j}^{2}\Delta_{j}/\log(T)\to\infty
, and the sharper rate of estimation.

Appendix B
Proofs

We define some notations used throughout the proof in addition to those given in Introduction.

For a random variable
X
and
\nu>0
, write
\|X\|_{\nu}=[\mathbb{E}(|X|^{\nu})]^{1/\nu}
.
Unless specified otherwise, we denote vectors, matrices and tensors by lower-case letters, capital letters, and calligraphic letters, i.e.
x
,
X
, and
\mathcal{X}
, respectively. We also use
x_{i},X_{ij},X_{i\cdot},X_{\cdot i}
to denote, respectively, the
i
-th element of a vector
x
, the
(i,j)
-th element of
X
, the
i
-th row vector (as a column vector) of
X
, and the
i
-th column vector of
X
.
We use
a\lesssim b
to denote
a=\mathcal{O}(b)
,
a\gtrsim b
to denote
b=\mathcal{O}(a)
, and
a\asymp b
to denote
a=\mathcal{O}(b)
and
b=\mathcal{O}(a)
; their versions of stochastic order, i.e. with
\mathcal{O}(\cdot)
replaced by
\mathcal{O}_{P}(\cdot)
, are respectively denoted by
a\lesssim_{P}b
,
b\gtrsim_{P}a
, and
a\asymp_{P}b
.
The
i
-th largest eigenvalue of a matrix
A
is denoted by
\lambda_{i}(A)
.
We write
\mathrm{rank}(A)
for the rank of a matrix
A
. For a square matrix
A
, we write
\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A)
for its trace.
Also, for a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
,
\mathrm{vec}(A)
denotes the vector in
\mathbb{R}^{mn}
obtained by stacking the columns of
A
in column-major order, with
|A|_{a}=\|\mathrm{vec}(A)\|_{a}
for
a\in\{1,2,\infty\}
.
We use
\otimes
to represent the Kronecker product. By convention, the total Kronecker product for an index set is computed in descending index.
We write
r=\prod_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}
and
r_{\text{-}k}=r/r_{k}
.

B.1
Proof of Theorem
1

Lemma B.1
.

Given any matrices
A,B\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, not necessarily of full rank, such that
AA^{\intercal}=cBB^{\intercal}
for some nonzero scalar
c
, we can express
B=c^{-1/2}AQ
for some orthogonal matrix
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

Both
AA^{\intercal}
and
BB^{\intercal}
are positive semi-definite, and the fact that
AA^{\intercal}=cBB^{\intercal}
implies that they have the same eigenvalues (up to scaling by
c
) and eigenspaces. Let
A=U\Sigma V^{\intercal}
be the SVD of
A
, where
U
and
V
are orthogonal matrices and
\Sigma
is diagonal. Then
AA^{\intercal}=U\Sigma^{2}U^{\intercal}
, and hence
BB^{\intercal}=c^{-1}U\Sigma^{2}U^{\intercal}
. The SVD of
B
is then
B=c^{-1/2}U\Sigma W
for some orthogonal matrix
W
, and with
AV=U\Sigma
, we conclude that
B=c^{-1/2}A(VW)
where
VW
is orthogonal, as required.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Under the model in (
2.2
) and Condition
1
,
A_{j,k}
can only be identified up to an orthogonal transformation for all
j\in[q+1]
and
k\in[K]
.

Proof.

Suppose there are two sets of parameters
\{\mathcal{F}_{t},A_{j,1},\dots,A_{j,K}\}
and
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t},\widetilde{A}_{j,1},\dots,\widetilde{A}_{j,K}\}
such that
\mathcal{F}_{t}\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{j,k}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\times_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{A}_{j,k}
. For identification, without loss of generality, fix
A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}=\widetilde{A}_{j,k}\widetilde{A}_{j,k}^{\intercal}=\Sigma_{j,k}
for some positive semi-definite matrix
\Sigma_{j,k}
. Taking any mode-
k
unfolding on both sides, we have

A_{j,k}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)^{\intercal}=\widetilde{A}_{j,k}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}\widetilde{A}_{j,i}\Big)^{\intercal}.

Thus, using Lemma
B.3
,

\displaystyle\quad A_{j,k}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big\}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big\{\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)\Big\}\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big\{\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}\widetilde{A}_{j,i}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}\widetilde{A}_{j,i}\Big)\Big\}\cdot\widetilde{A}_{j,k}\widetilde{A}_{j,k}^{\intercal}.

Under the trivial condition that any
A_{j,i}
for
j\in[q+1]
and
i\in[K]
, is not a zero matrix, the above shows that we can write
\widetilde{A}_{j,k}\widetilde{A}_{j,k}^{\intercal}=cA_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}
for some nonzero constant
c
. Using Lemma
B.1
, we have
\widetilde{A}_{j,k}=c^{1/2}A_{j,k}Q
for some orthogonal matrix
Q
. Then we conclude that
c=1
since

\Sigma_{j,k}=\widetilde{A}_{j,k}\widetilde{A}_{j,k}^{\intercal}=cA_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}=c\Sigma_{j,k}.

This shows that
A_{j,k}
can only be identified up to an orthogonal transformation.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1
.

First, by inspecting the proof of Lemma
B.3
, we note that statement (
B.1
) remains to hold without Assumption
1
—all we need is Condition
1
.
Also by Lemma
B.2
,
A_{j,k}
can only be identified up to an orthogonal transformation.

Next, for any
j\in[q]
, we show that a change of the form
A_{j,\ell}=cA_{j+1,\ell}
, for some positive constant
c
(without loss of generality, due to the identification), is mode-unidentifiable. This is trivial by noting that we can write

\mathcal{F}_{t}\times_{k\in[K]\setminus\{\ell\}}A_{j,k}\times_{\ell}(cA_{j,\ell})=\mathcal{F}_{t}\times_{k\in[K]\setminus\{\ell\}}(c^{1/(K-1)}A_{j+1,k})\times_{\ell}A_{j,\ell}.

Finally, let a change from
A_{j,k}
to
A_{j+1,k}
be an arbitrary mode-unidentifiable change. It remains to show the change is in the form of a scalar multiple. By Definition
2
, there exists a set of parameters
\{B_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}

such that

\displaystyle\quad A_{j+1,k}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\Big(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}A_{j+1,\ell}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}A_{j+1,\ell}\Big)\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big\}A_{j+1,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big\{\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}A_{j+1,\ell}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}A_{j+1,\ell}\Big)\Big\}\cdot A_{j+1,k}A_{j+1,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big\{\Big(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}B_{\ell}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}B_{\ell}\Big)\Big\}\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal},

which again follows from Lemma
B.3
. Hence by Lemma
B.1
, we conclude that
A_{j+1,k}=cA_{j,k}Q
for some nonzero constant
c
and orthogonal matrix
Q
, and this completes the proof of the theorem by noting that any
A_{j,k}
is identified up to an arbitrary rotation.
∎

B.2
Consistency in loading estimation

Throughout, we denote the global mode-
k
covariance matrix for the pseudo-factor
\mathcal{G}_{t}
and its sample version, and the (scaled) covariance matrices for
\mathcal{X}_{t}
as follows.

\displaystyle{\Gamma}_{G}^{(k)}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big\},\quad\widehat{\Gamma}_{G}^{(k)}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})^{\intercal},

\displaystyle{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}=\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{t})\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big\}.

B.2.1
Supporting lemmas

Throughout this subsection, anytime we refer to Assumptions
1
and
4
, we assume
(\nu,\beta)=(4,0)
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let Assumption
1
hold. Then for any
k\in[K]
,
t\in[T]
, and constant matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{\text{-}k}\times r_{\text{-}k}}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big\}=\textnormal{\text{tr}}(M)\,I_{r_{k}}.

(B.1)

For any interval of timestamps
\mathcal{S}
, if it further holds that
\|M\|_{F}^{2}=o(|\mathcal{S}|)
, then

\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{S}}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}\xrightarrow{P}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(M)\,I_{r_{k}}.

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

Consider the
(i,j)
-th entry of
\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}
. If
i\neq j
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}\big\}_{i,j}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{i\cdot}^{\intercal}\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{j\cdot}\Big]=0,

where the last equality used Assumption
1
that entries of
\mathcal{F}_{t}
are cross-sectionally uncorrelated. For the diagonal entries, i.e.
i=j
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})^{\intercal}\big\}_{i,i}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{i\cdot}^{\intercal}\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{i\cdot}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{i,1}M_{1,1}+\dots+\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{i,r_{k}}M_{r_{k},r_{k}}=\textnormal{\text{tr}}(M),

where the second line also used Assumption
1
. Hence (
B.1
) is true.

To show the second result, it suffices to show the result for
\mathcal{S}=[T]
and from its proof the similar argument follows for any interval
\mathcal{S}
. To this end, note that by Assumption
1
, there exist innovation
\mathcal{X}_{f,t}
and coefficients
a_{f,w}
as in Definition
3
such that
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{f,w}\mathcal{X}_{f,t-w}
. Then consider first that for any
t
,

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{s=1}^{T}\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{f,w}a_{f,w-(t-s)}\Big)\Big(\sum_{z\geq 0}a_{f,z}a_{f,z-(t-s)}\Big)\\
&\leq\Big(\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{w\geq 0}|a_{f,w}||a_{f,w-(t-s)}|\Big)\Big(\sum_{z\geq 0}|a_{f,z}|\Big)\cdot\max_{z}|a_{f,z}|=\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}|a_{f,w}|\Big)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1),\end{split}

(B.2)

where the last two equalities used the decaying coefficients in Definition
3
. Similarly, it holds that

\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{f,w}^{2}a_{f,w-(t-s)}^{2}\leq\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{f,w}^{2}\Big)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1).

(B.3)

Finally, with the notation
X_{f,t,(k)}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{f,t})
, for any
i,j\in[r_{k}]
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\Big(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{i\cdot}^{\intercal}\,M\,\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{j\cdot}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\Big(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{l=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{v=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{il}M_{lv}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{jv}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{l=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{v=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{w\geq 0}\sum_{z\geq 0}a_{f,w}a_{f,z}X_{f,t-w,(k),il}M_{lv}X_{f,t-z,(k),jv},

\displaystyle\quad\quad\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{g=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{u=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{h\geq 0}\sum_{m\geq 0}a_{f,h}a_{f,m}X_{f,s-h,(k),ig}M_{gu}X_{f,s-m,(k),ju}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{f,w}a_{f,w-(t-s)}\Big)\Big(\sum_{z\geq 0}a_{f,z}a_{f,z-(t-s)}\Big)\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{v=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}M_{lv}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{w\geq 0}\sum_{z\geq 0}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{h\geq 0}\sum_{m\geq 0}a_{f,w}a_{f,z}a_{f,h}a_{f,m}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}M_{ll}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\Big(X_{f,t-w,(k),il}X_{f,t-z,(k),il},X_{f,s-h,(k),il}X_{f,s-m,(k),il}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{T}\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{v=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}M_{lv}^{2}\Big)+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{T}\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{r_{\text{-}k}}M_{ll}^{2}\Big)=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{T}\Big)\cdot\|M\|_{F}^{2}=o(1),

where the third equality considered
i=j
and
i\neq j
separately, and the fourth used (
B.2
) and (
B.3
). Combining the above with (
B.1
), the proof of the lemma is completed.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

(i)

For any square matrix
M
and sequence of matrices
A_{t},\,t\in[T]
, of compatible dimensions, we have

\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}MA_{t}^{\intercal}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq\|M\|^{2}\cdot\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}A_{t}^{\intercal}\right\|_{F}^{2}.

Note that this inequality is tight, which can be seen by taking
M
as the identity matrix.

(ii)

(Lemma B.16 in
10
) For any sequence of matrices
A_{t}=[A_{t,ij}]
,
B_{t}
for
t\in[T]
, and any matrix
M
of compatible dimensions, we have

\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}MB_{t}^{\intercal}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq\|M\|_{F}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i,j}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t,ij}B_{t}\right\|_{F}^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

Part (ii) is a direct quotation of Lemma B.16 in
10
, so it is enough to show part (i) below. Expanding the squared Frobenius norm, we have

\begin{split}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}MA_{t}^{\intercal}\right\|_{F}^{2}&=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\left\{\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}MA_{t}^{\intercal}\right)\left(\sum_{s=1}^{T}A_{s}M^{\intercal}A_{s}^{\intercal}\right)\right\}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{t}MA_{t}^{\intercal}A_{s}M^{\intercal}A_{s}^{\intercal})\\
&=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{s}^{\intercal}A_{t}MA_{t}^{\intercal}A_{s}M^{\intercal})=:\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{st}MA_{st}^{\intercal}M^{\intercal}),\end{split}

(B.4)

where the second line used the cyclic property of trace, and the definition
A_{st}=A_{s}^{\intercal}A_{t}
. For any
t,s\in[T]
, we may read
\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{st}MA_{st}^{\intercal}M^{\intercal})
as a Frobenius inner product and apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality such that

\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{st}MA_{st}^{\intercal}M^{\intercal})=\langle A_{st}M,MA_{st}\rangle_{F}\leq\|A_{st}M\|_{F}\cdot\|MA_{st}\|_{F}\leq\|M\|^{2}\|A_{st}\|_{F}^{2},

(B.5)

where the last inequality used the fact that for any matrix products
AB
, we have
\|AB\|_{F}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|A_{i\cdot}^{\intercal}B\|^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|A_{i\cdot}^{\intercal}\|^{2}\|B\|^{2}}=\|A\|_{F}\cdot\|B\|
.

Combining (
B.4
) and (
B.5
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}MA_{t}^{\intercal}\right\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{st}MA_{st}^{\intercal}M^{\intercal})\leq\|M\|^{2}\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\|A_{st}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|M\|^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{s}^{\intercal}A_{t}A_{t}^{\intercal}A_{s})=\|M\|^{2}\cdot\textnormal{\text{tr}}\left\{\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}A_{t}^{\intercal}\right)\left(\sum_{s=1}^{T}A_{s}A_{s}^{\intercal}\right)\right\}=\|M\|^{2}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}A_{t}^{\intercal}\right\|_{F}^{2},

as desired. This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

For ease of notation, for any
t\in[T]
,
k\in[K]
,
j\in[q+1]
, denote

\begin{split}&X_{k,t}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{t}),\quad G_{k,t}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t}),\quad E_{k,t}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{E}_{t}),\quad F_{k,t}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{t}),\\
&\Lambda_{\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\Lambda_{\ell},\quad A_{j,\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}A_{j,\ell}.\end{split}

(B.6)

Lemma B.5
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,
’
‣
A.3.1
, and
4
,
we have the following for any
k\in[K]
and
j\in[q+1]
.

(i)

\Big\|\sum_{t=a+1}^{b}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\Lambda_{\ell})^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{(b-a)p_{k}^{2}p_{\text{-}k}\Big\}
for any integers
a\leq b
; in particular,
\Big\|\sum_{t=\theta_{j-1}+1}^{\theta_{j}}(\Lambda_{k}A_{j,k})F_{k,t}(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\Lambda_{\ell}A_{j,\ell})^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})p_{k}^{2}p_{\text{-}k}\Big\}
;

(ii)

\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(Tp_{k}^{2}p_{\text{-}k}+T^{2}p_{k}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big)
, where in particular the first term is attributed to the bound on
\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big\|\big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\big\{E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}-\mathbb{E}(E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal})\big\}\big)^{2}\big\|_{F}\Big\}
, and the second term on
\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big)\Big\|_{F}^{2}
; moreover,
\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big)\Big\|^{2}\leq cT^{2}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}
for some constant
c>0
;

(iii)

\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\Lambda_{k,i\cdot}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}E_{k,t,z\cdot}\Big\|^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(Tp+T^{2}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big)
for any
z\in[p_{k}]
;

(iv)

\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{4})\Big(Tp_{k}^{2}+T^{2}p_{k}\Big)\Big\}
for any
V
independent of
\mathcal{E}_{t}
;

(v)

\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k,z\cdot}E_{k,t}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(Tp)
for any
i\in[p_{k}]
,
z\in[p_{\text{-}k}]
.

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.

For part (i), we only show the special case

\Bigg\|\sum_{t=\theta_{j-1}+1}^{\theta_{j}}(\Lambda_{k}A_{j,k})F_{k,t}(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\Lambda_{\ell}A_{j,\ell})^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})p_{k}^{2}p_{\text{-}k}\Big\},

while the general statement follows in exactly the same way. To do this, we apply the first result of Lemma 3 in
15
with all factors being pervasive therein, observing that: (1) their results continue to hold even if the loading matrices have deficient column rank (
\Lambda_{\ell}A_{j,\ell}
in our case); (2) the factor model is considered segment by segment here and hence the total length is replaced by each interval length
\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1}
; and (3) all other related assumptions are satisfied by our Assumptions
1
,
2
,
4
and
’
‣
A.3.1
, except that we only need independent innovation process in our Definition
3
but their results remain to hold from the proof. Then result is then direct. Similarly, the first claim in part (ii) is by applying the second result of Lemma 3 in
15
, together with its proof. For the second claim in part (ii), note that

\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big)\Big\|_{1}^{2}=\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big)\Big\|_{\infty}^{2}=\mathcal{O}\Big(T^{2}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big),

where the second equality is direct from Proposition 1.1 in the supplement of
16
.
Then the claim holds by Hölder’s inequality.

Consider part (iii), and for simplicity, let
\mathcal{IV}_{\Lambda,j}=\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\Lambda_{k,i\cdot}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Big\|^{2}
. We will leverage the proof of Lemma 5 in
15
, where their term
\mathcal{I}_{3,j}
is exactly our
\mathcal{IV}_{\Lambda,j}
scaled by
1/p_{k}
due to the different normalization of the loading matrix. To verify the related conditions are fulfilled, note that our set of Assumption
2
and Assumption
4
are the same as their Assumptions (L1), (L2), (E1), and (E2), except that we only require independent innovation process in our Definition
3
while they require
i.i.d.
innovation in their parallel conditions. However, from investigating their proof of Lemma 5, the results remain to hold if the innovations are not of the same distribution. Together with the fact that all our factors are strong and hence their Assumption (R1) trivially holds, we may directly apply their result on
\mathcal{I}_{3,j}
, so that as desired:

\mathcal{IV}_{\Lambda,j}=p_{k}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(Tp_{\text{-}k}+T^{2}p_{k}^{-1}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big)=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(Tp+T^{2}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big).

Next, consider part (iv). Note that by Assumption
2
(i), we have

\begin{split}\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}&=\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{p_{k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Lambda_{k,j\cdot}^{\intercal}\Big\|^{2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{p_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Big)^{2}.\end{split}

(B.7)

For any
i,j\in[p_{k}]
, consider first
\mathbb{E}\big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\big)
.
Under Assumption
4
, there exist innovation processes
\{\mathcal{X}_{e,t}\}
,
\{\mathcal{X}_{\epsilon,t}\}
and coefficients
\{a_{e,w}\}
,
\{a_{\epsilon,h}\}
as in Definition
3
such that

\mathcal{E}_{t}=\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{e,w}\mathcal{X}_{e,t-w}\Big)\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{e,k}+\Sigma_{\epsilon}\circ\Big(\sum_{h\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,h}\mathcal{X}_{\epsilon,t-h}\Big).

Then it holds immediately for any
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle E_{k,t}=\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{e,w}A_{e,k}X_{e,t-w,k}A_{e,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+\sum_{h\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,h}\Big(\Sigma_{\epsilon,k}\circ X_{\epsilon,t-h,k}\Big),\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle X_{e,t-w,k}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{e,t-w}),\;X_{\epsilon,t-h,k}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{\epsilon,t-h}),\;A_{e,\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}A_{e,\ell},\;\Sigma_{\epsilon,k}=\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\Sigma_{\epsilon}).

Then we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{e,w}A_{e,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}X_{e,t-w,k}A_{e,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+\sum_{h\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,h}\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}\circ X_{\epsilon,t-h,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}\Big)VV^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{e,w}A_{e,\text{-}k}X_{e,t-w,k}^{\intercal}A_{e,k,j\cdot}+\sum_{h\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,h}\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,j\cdot}\circ X_{\epsilon,t-h,k,j\cdot}\Big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{w\geq 0}\sum_{m\geq 0}a_{e,w}a_{e,m}A_{e,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}X_{e,t-w,k}A_{e,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}A_{e,\text{-}k}X_{e,t-m,k}^{\intercal}A_{e,k,j\cdot}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h\geq 0}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,h}a_{\epsilon,\ell}(\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}\circ X_{\epsilon,t-h,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal})VV^{\intercal}(\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,j\cdot}\circ X_{\epsilon,t-\ell,k,j\cdot})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{\text{tr}}(A_{e,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}A_{e,\text{-}k})]\cdot T\cdot A_{e,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}A_{e,k,j\cdot}+\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{\text{tr}}(VV^{\intercal})]\cdot T\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{\{i=j\}},

so that together with the sparsity of
A_{e,\ell}
,
\ell\in[K]
, from Assumption
4
,

\sum_{j=1}^{p_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Big)\Big\}^{2}\lesssim T^{2}\cdot[\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{2})]^{2}\cdot p_{k}.

(B.8)

Next, for any
i,j\in[p_{k}]
, consider
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\big)
and we may simplify

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Big)=\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{h=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}E_{k,t,i\ell}(VV^{\intercal})_{\ell h}E_{k,t,jh}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\Big\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{h=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}(VV^{\intercal})_{\ell h}\Big(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{e,w}A_{e,k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}X_{e,t-w,k}A_{e,\text{-}k,\ell\cdot}+\sum_{m\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,m}\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,i\ell}X_{\epsilon,t-m,k,i\ell}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\sum_{c\geq 0}a_{e,c}A_{e,k,j\cdot}^{\intercal}X_{e,t-c,k}A_{e,\text{-}k,h\cdot}+\sum_{z\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,z}\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,jh}X_{\epsilon,t-z,k,jh}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{h=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}\mathbb{E}[(VV^{\intercal})_{\ell h}^{2}]\left(\sum_{w\geq 0}a_{e,w}^{2}\left\|A_{e,k,i\cdot}\right\|^{2}\left\|A_{e,\text{-}k,\ell\cdot}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{m\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,m}^{2}\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,i\ell}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\sum_{c\geq 0}a_{e,c}^{2}\left\|A_{e,k,j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\left\|A_{e,\text{-}k,h\cdot}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{z\geq 0}a_{\epsilon,z}^{2}\Sigma_{\epsilon,k,jh}^{2}\right)\lesssim T\cdot\mathbb{E}(\|VV^{\intercal}\|_{F}^{2})\leq T\cdot\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{4}).

Hence, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{j=1}^{p_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t,j\cdot}\Big)\lesssim Tp_{k}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{4}).\end{split}

(B.9)

Combining (
B.7
), (
B.8
) and (
B.9
), we have the desired:

\mathbb{E}\Big(\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}VV^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\Big)\lesssim T^{2}p_{k}\cdot[\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{2})]^{2}+Tp_{k}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{4})\leq(T^{2}p_{k}+Tp_{k}^{2})\cdot\mathbb{E}(\|V\|_{F}^{4}).

This completes the proof of claim (iv). For part (v), it is similar to part (i) and direct from the proof of Lemma 3 in
15
.
∎

Lemma B.6
.

We have the following.

(i)

Let Assumption
3
(i)–(ii) hold. Then Assumption
3
(iii)–(iv) implies Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(iii). In fact, conditional on Assumption
3
(iv), its part (iii) is a necessary and sufficient condition for
\Sigma_{A,k}
to be positive definite.

(ii)

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
and
4
hold. Further let Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1

hold. For any
k\in[K]
, let
\widetilde{D}_{k}=\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{\intercal}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}/p_{k}
be the diagonal matrix consisting of the leading
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
. Then as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
, the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{D}_{k}
satisfy
\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{D}_{k})=\lambda_{i}(\Sigma_{A,k})+o_{P}(1)
for any
i\leq r_{k}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.6
.

We first show result (i), for which it is sufficient to show that given Assumption
3
(iv), its part (iii) is a necessary and sufficient condition for
\Sigma_{A,k}
to be positive definite. To see this, note

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\|A_{j,\text{-}k}\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}=\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}c_{j}A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

(B.10)

is a linear combination of
A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}
over
j\in[q+1]
where
c_{j}=\|A_{j,\text{-}k}\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})/T
which is a positive constant. Then conditional on part (iv) in Assumption
3
, we have Assumption
3
(iii) if and only if Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(iii) holds, as shown below.

“
\Rightarrow
”:

Fix any
k\in[K]
, the quadratic form of (
B.10
) satisfies, for any nonzero
u\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}}
,

\displaystyle\quad u^{\intercal}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}c_{j}A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}\Big)u

\displaystyle=u^{\intercal}[A_{1,k},A_{2,k},\dots,A_{q+1,k}]\,\mathrm{diag}(c_{1},c_{2},\dots,c_{q+1})\,\big\{u^{\intercal}[A_{1,k},A_{2,k},\dots,A_{q+1,k}]\big\}^{\intercal},

which is positive as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
, since
[A_{1,k},\dots,A_{q+1,k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times(q+1)r_{k}}
is asymptotically of full row rank from Assumption
3
(iii), and
c_{j}
is uniformly bounded away from zero over
j\in[q+1]
from Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(i) and Assumption
3
(iv). Thus,
\Sigma_{A,k}
is positive definite.

“
\Leftarrow
”:

As
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
, if
[A_{1,k},\dots,A_{q+1,k}]
does not have full row rank, then there must exists some nonzero
u\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}}
making the quadratic form in the above “
\Rightarrow
” part zero, and hence
\Sigma_{A,k}
cannot be positive definite.

Next, we show result (ii) of the lemma. By (
2.2
) and the definition of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
, we have

\begin{split}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}&=\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{k,t}X_{k,t}^{\intercal}=\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+E_{k,t})(\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+E_{k,t})^{\intercal}\\
&=\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}=:\mathcal{I}+\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}+\mathcal{IV}.\end{split}

(B.11)

We first show find the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{I}
. Using (
2.2
), any mode-
k
unfolding of
\mathcal{G}_{t}
is read as

\displaystyle G_{k,t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}A_{j,k}F_{k,t}A_{j,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{\theta_{j-1}<t\leq\theta_{j}\}},

so that immediately, as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}=\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}A_{j,k}F_{k,t}A_{j,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}A_{j,\text{-}k}F_{k,t}^{\intercal}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{\theta_{j-1}<t\leq\theta_{j}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})A_{j,k}\Big\{\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j-1}+1}^{\theta_{j}}F_{k,t}A_{j,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Big(\frac{\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}}{p_{\text{-}k}}\Big)A_{j,\text{-}k}F_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{P}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\|A_{j,\text{-}k}\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}\to\Sigma_{A,k},

(B.12)

where the last line used Assumption
2
, Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(i)–(ii), and Lemma
B.3
. Together with Assumption
’
‣
A.3.1
(iii) (or Assumption
3
(iii)–(iv), according to part (a) of this lemma), we have

\mathcal{I}\xrightarrow{P}\frac{\Lambda_{k}\Sigma_{A,k}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}}{p_{k}},

which has the leading
r_{k}
eigenvalues asymptotically equal to those of
\Sigma_{A,k}
by Assumption
2
again. Hence
\lambda_{i}(\mathcal{I})=\lambda_{i}(\Sigma_{A,k})+o_{P}(1)
for all
i\leq r_{k}
, while
\lambda_{i}(\mathcal{I})=0
for
i>r_{k}
by
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{I})\leq r_{k}
.

Next, consider the squared Frobenius norm of
\mathcal{II}
in (
B.11
). If we re-write

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{II}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}(\Lambda_{k}A_{j,k})F_{k,t}(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\Lambda_{\ell}A_{j,\ell})^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{\theta_{j-1}<t\leq\theta_{j}\}}\Big\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\Big\|\sum_{t=\theta_{j-1}+1}^{\theta_{j}}(\Lambda_{k}A_{j,k})F_{k,t}(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\Lambda_{\ell}A_{j,\ell})^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})p_{k}^{2}p_{\text{-}k}\Big\}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{\frac{Tpp_{k}}{(Tp)^{2}}\Big\}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\Big),

where the last line used Lemma
B.5
(i). The above is also the same rate for
\|\mathcal{III}\|_{F}^{2}
by exactly the same arguments. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{IV}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}}\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(Tp_{k}^{2}p_{\text{-}k}+T^{2}p_{k}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big)=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big),

where the last line used Lemma
B.5
(ii). Combining all the above and applying Weyl’s inequality iteratively, the first
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
are led by those of
\mathcal{I}
, among the decomposition in (
B.11
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

Let all the assumptions in Lemma
B.6
(ii) hold. For any
k\in[K]
, with
\widetilde{D}_{k}
from Lemma
B.6
, define

\widetilde{H}_{k}=\frac{1}{Tp}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big)\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}.

Then
\widetilde{H}_{k}
is asymptotically invertible as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
and, moreover, satisfies
\widetilde{H}_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}^{\intercal}=I_{r_{k}}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(1/\sqrt{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+1/p_{k})
. Also, the initial estimator
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}
satisfies that

\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p_{k}^{2}}\Big).

Proof of Lemma
B.7
.

By the definitions of eigenvalues and eigenvectors, we have
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}
and hence
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}
by the fact that
\widetilde{D}_{k}
is asymptotically invertible from Lemma
B.6
, which also spells out
\|\widetilde{D}_{k}\|_{F}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
and
\|\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}\|_{F}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
. Moreover, note that with
\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}
replaced by
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}
in (
B.12
), the arguments therein remain exactly the same
and hence for the matrix
\widetilde{H}_{k}
, we have
\|\widetilde{H}_{k}\|_{F}\asymp_{P}\|\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\|_{F}/p_{k}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
which used
\|\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}=\mathcal{O}(p_{k}^{1/2})
from Assumption
2
and
\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\|_{F}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(p_{k}^{1/2})
from its definition. All the above auxiliary results are summarized below:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left\{\|\widetilde{D}_{k}\|_{F},\;\|\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}\|_{F},\;\|\widetilde{H}_{k}\|_{F}\right\}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)\text{ \ and \ }\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left\{\|\Lambda_{k}\|_{F},\;\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\|_{F}\right\}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(p_{k}^{1/2}).}

(B.13)

With (
B.11
), we have
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}=(\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}+\mathcal{IV})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}
. Thus using (
B.13
),

\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}+\mathcal{IV})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\\
&\lesssim_{P}\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}+\mathcal{IV})\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}+\mathcal{IV})(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k})\Big\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}+\mathcal{IV})\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\Big\}+o_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\Big),\end{split}

(B.14)

where the last equality used the rates for
\|\mathcal{II}\|_{F}^{2}
,
\|\mathcal{III}\|_{F}^{2}
, and
\|\mathcal{IV}\|_{F}^{2}
in the proof of Lemma
B.6
.

For the first term in the last line of (
B.14
), note immediately by the rates for
\|\mathcal{II}\|_{F}^{2}
and
\|\mathcal{III}\|_{F}^{2}
in the proof of Lemma
B.6
and (
B.13
) that

\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III})\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\Big(\|\mathcal{II}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\mathcal{III}\|_{F}^{2}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{p_{k}}\|\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\Big).

(B.15)

Next, we study
\|\mathcal{IV}\cdot\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}/p_{k}
. To this end, we write

\begin{split}\frac{1}{p_{k}}\|\mathcal{IV}\cdot\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}&=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\sum_{z=1}^{p_{k}}\Big\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k})_{z\cdot}\Big\|^{2}\\
&=\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}p_{k}}\sum_{z=1}^{p_{k}}\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\Lambda_{k,i\cdot}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t,i\cdot}^{\intercal}E_{k,t,z\cdot}\Big\|^{2}\\
&=\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}p_{k}}\sum_{z=1}^{p_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(Tp+T^{2}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}\Big)=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp}+\frac{1}{p_{k}^{2}}\Big).\end{split}

(B.16)

where the second last equality used Lemma
B.5
(iii).

Combining (
B.14
), (
B.15
) and (
B.16
), we conclude
\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}/p_{k}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1/(Tp_{\text{-}k})+1/p_{k}^{2})
, as desired. To end the proof of the lemma, it remains to show
\widetilde{H}_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}^{\intercal}=I_{r_{k}}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(1/\sqrt{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+1/p_{k})
and hence
\widetilde{H}_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\xrightarrow{P}I_{r_{k}}
, which follows from that

\displaystyle I_{r_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{\intercal}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k})+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{\intercal}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k})+\frac{1}{p_{k}}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k})^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\widetilde{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}^{\intercal}+\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{Tp_{\text{-}k}}}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big),

where the last equality used Assumption
2
, (
B.13
) and the rate of
\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k}\|_{F}
.
∎

Lemma B.8
.

Let all the assumptions in Lemma
B.6
(ii) hold. For any
k\in[K]
, with
\mathcal{I}
from (
B.11
), define

\begin{split}\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}&=\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal})=:\mathcal{I}+{\Gamma}_{E}^{(k)}.\end{split}

(B.17)

Let
\ddot{\Lambda}_{k}
be
\sqrt{p_{k}}
times the
r_{k}
leading eigenvectors of
\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
. Then as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
, there exists orthogonal matrix
J_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times r_{k}}
such that

\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{k}J_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\Big).

Proof of Lemma
B.8
.

For our proof, we will adapt Lemma B.4 (i) in
10
, by equating our
p_{k}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
,
p_{k}\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
, and
p_{k}\mathcal{I}
as their
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}
, and
\mathbf{S}
, respectively. Instead of verifying the exact condition (C1) in
10
, we note that the eigenvalues required therein need not be necessarily distinct, in which case the diagonal matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}
in their Lemma B.4 (i) is compromised to some orthogonal matrix, say matrix
J_{k}
. It suffices to show
p_{k}^{-1}
times the first
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
p_{k}\mathcal{I}
, or equivalently, the first
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
\mathcal{I}
, are bounded away from zero and infinity, which is true from the proof of Lemma
B.6
(ii).

It remains to verify the conditions (C2) and (C3) in Lemma B.4 in
10
. For the condition (C2), using the decomposition of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
in (
B.11
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}p_{k}^{-2}\Big\|p_{k}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}-p_{k}\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}\Big\|_{F}^{2}&=\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big\{E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}-\mathbb{E}(E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal})\Big\}+\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=:\Big\|\ddot{\mathcal{IV}}+\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}\Big\|_{F}^{2}.\end{split}

By the first claim in Lemma
B.5
(ii), we have
\|\ddot{\mathcal{IV}}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1/Tp_{\text{-}k})
, the rate of which applies also to
\|\mathcal{II}\|_{F}^{2}
and
\|\mathcal{III}\|_{F}^{2}
from the proof of Lemma
B.6
. Thus, the term
\zeta_{n,p}
in condition (C2) is
1/\sqrt{Tp_{\text{-}k}}
here. Lastly, condition (C3) is direct by noting that (
B.17
) gives

\Big\|p_{k}\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}-p_{k}\mathcal{I}\Big\|=\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal})\Big\|=\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}\mathcal{O}\Big(Tp_{\text{-}k}\Big)=\mathcal{O}(1),

where the second equality used the second claim in Lemma
B.5
(ii). With the relevant conditions in Lemma B.4 of
10
verified, and with the fact that
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}
and
\ddot{\Lambda}_{k}
are
\sqrt{p_{k}}
times the first
r_{k}
eigenvectors of
p_{k}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
and
p_{k}\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
or equivalently, eigenvectors of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
and
\ddot{\Gamma}_{X}^{(k)}
, we conclude that there exists orthogonal matrix
J_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times r_{k}}
such that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{k}}}\Big\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{k}J_{k}\Big\|_{F}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{Tp_{\text{-}k}}}\Big),

as desired. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma B.9
.

Let all the assumptions in Lemma
B.6
(ii) hold.
For any
k\in[K]
, recall
\widehat{D}_{k}
from Lemma
B.10
.
Then as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
, the eigenvalues of
\widehat{D}_{k}
satisfy
\lambda_{i}(\widehat{D}_{k})=\lambda_{i}(\Sigma_{A,k})+o_{P}(1)
for any
i\leq r_{k}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.9
.

From the definition of
Y_{k,t}
in (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}_{Y}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Tp_{k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{k,t}Y_{k,t}^{\intercal}=\frac{1}{Tp_{k}p_{\text{-}k}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{t})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{t})^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+E_{k,t})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}(\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+E_{k,t})^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}.

(B.18)

We first present a preliminary result based on Lemma
B.7
and Assumption
2
. That is, for any
\ell\in[K]
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}^{\intercal}=\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}-\Lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}\Lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}^{\intercal}\\
&=\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}-\Lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}\Lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}-\Lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell})^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}\Lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell}\widetilde{H}_{\ell}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\ell}^{\intercal}=\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}\Lambda_{\ell}\Lambda_{\ell}^{\intercal}+o_{P}(1),\end{split}

(B.19)

noting that

\Lambda_{\ell}\Lambda_{\ell}^{\intercal}/p_{\ell}
has the first
r_{k}
eigenvalues being 1’s and the rest being zero. Immediately, we also have
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}/p_{\text{-}k}=\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}/p_{\text{-}k}+o_{P}(1)
. Hence for
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}
, similar to (
B.12
),

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}G_{k,t}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})A_{j,k}\Big\{\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j-1}+1}^{\theta_{j}}F_{k,t}A_{j,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Big(\frac{\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}}{p_{\text{-}k}^{2}}\Big)A_{j,\text{-}k}F_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{P}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})A_{j,k}\Big\{\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j-1}+1}^{\theta_{j}}F_{k,t}A_{j,\text{-}k}^{\intercal}A_{j,\text{-}k}F_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Big\}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{P}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\|A_{j,\text{-}k}\|_{F}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{j-1})\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}\to\Sigma_{A,k}.

The remaining arguments on
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}
is exactly the same as those on
\mathcal{I}
in Lemma
B.6
, so that
\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}})=\lambda_{i}(\Sigma_{A,k})+o_{P}(1)
for all
i\leq r_{k}
, while
\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}})=0
for
i>r_{k}
by
\mathrm{rank}(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}})\leq r_{k}
.

Before working on
\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}
, with the notation in Lemma
B.8
, define
\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\ell}
and
J_{\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}J_{\ell}
. Then similar to (
B.19
), it holds for any
k\in[K]
that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}&=\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\\
&=\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k})^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\\
&=\frac{1}{p_{\text{-}k}}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{w_{\text{-}k}}),\end{split}

(B.20)

where in the last equality, we used
w_{\text{-}k}=\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}1/(Tp_{\text{-}\ell})
and applied Lemma
B.8
to

\Bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}}{\sqrt{p_{\text{-}k}}}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}=\Bigg\|\Bigg(\otimes_{h\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{h}}{\sqrt{p_{h}}}\Bigg)-\Bigg(\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{\ddot{\Lambda}_{\ell}J_{\ell}}{\sqrt{p_{\ell}}}\Bigg)\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{1}{p_{\ell}}\Big\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\ell}J_{\ell}\Big\|_{F}^{2},

where the second equality can be easily shown and details are omitted here (see
e.g.
the induction argument in the proof of Lemma 6 in
15
,
15
).

Then consider
\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}
, for which we can also write

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{p_{\text{-}k}}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\frac{\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{p_{\text{-}k}}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}+w_{\text{-}k}\cdot\frac{1}{(Tp)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{k}}\sum_{z=1}^{p_{\text{-}k}}\Bigg\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k,i\cdot}^{\intercal}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k,z\cdot}E_{k,t}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{P}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda_{k}G_{k,t}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\frac{\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{p_{\text{-}k}}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}+w_{\text{-}k}\cdot\frac{1}{T}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{w_{\text{-}k}}{T}\Big),

where the second line used (
B.20
) and Lemma
B.4
(ii), and the third used Lemma
B.5
(v) and the proof of Proposition 1.2 (in their supplement) in
16
with Hölder’s inequality and Assumption
2
(i). Similarly, we have
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1/Tp_{\text{-}k}+w_{\text{-}k}/T)
.

For
\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}
, using (
B.19
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{p_{\text{-}k}}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\frac{\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{p_{\text{-}k}}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}+o_{P}\Bigg(\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{P}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\Big(\frac{\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{p_{\text{-}k}}-I_{r_{\text{-}k}}\Big)E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}+\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}=\|\mathcal{IV}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big),

where the last line used Lemma
B.4
(i),
\|\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}/p_{\text{-}k}-I_{r_{\text{-}k}}\|=\mathcal{O}(1)
, and the result on
\mathcal{IV}
in the proof of Lemma
B.6
. Combining Weyl’s inequality and the above results on the decomposition of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{Y}^{(k)}
in (
B.18
), the proof of this lemma is complete.
∎

B.2.2
Proof of Proposition
1

To show Proposition
1
, it suffices to prove the following lemma thanks to Lemma
B.6
(i); the lemma below also provides the explicit expression for
\widehat{H}_{k}
.

Lemma B.10
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
,
’
‣
A.3.1
and
4
hold, with
\nu\geq 4
and
\beta\geq 0
.
For any
k\in[K]
, let
\widehat{D}_{k}:=\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}^{\intercal}\widehat{\Gamma}_{Y}^{(k)}\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}/p_{k}
be the diagonal matrix consisting of the leading
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{Y}^{(k)}
.
Define

\widehat{H}_{k}:=\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{t})^{\intercal}\Big)\Lambda_{k}^{\intercal}\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}\widehat{D}_{k}^{-1}.

Then
\widehat{H}_{k}
is asymptotically invertible such that as
\min\{T,p_{1},\dots,p_{K}\}\to\infty
, we have

\widehat{H}_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}=I_{r_{k}}+\mathcal{O}_{P}\Bigg(\Big\{\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}+\Big(\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}\ell}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{T}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}\Bigg).

Moreover, the projection estimator
\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}
satisfies that

\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}+\Big(\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}\ell}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{T}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)\right).

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
B.7
, we have
\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{Y}^{(k)}\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}\widehat{D}_{k}^{-1}
from the definition, and hence
\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}=(\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}})\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}\widehat{D}_{k}^{-1}
from (
B.18
). Similar to (
B.13
),

\begin{split}\max\left\{\|\widehat{D}_{k}\|_{F},\;\|\widehat{D}_{k}^{-1}\|_{F},\;\|\widehat{H}_{k}\|_{F}\right\}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1),\quad\text{and}\quad\|\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}\|_{F}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(p_{k}^{1/2})&,\end{split}

(B.21)

where
\|\widehat{H}_{k}\|_{F}
is similar to
\|\widetilde{H}_{k}\|_{F}
from Lemma
B.7
, since we have
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}/p_{\text{-}k}=\Lambda_{\text{-}k}\Lambda_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}/p_{\text{-}k}+o_{P}(1)
by applying (
B.19
) on all but the
k
-th modes. Thus,

\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}})\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}\widehat{D}_{k}^{-1}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\\
&\lesssim_{P}\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}})\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}})(\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k})\Big\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|(\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}})\Lambda_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\right)+o_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

(B.22)

With the rates derived for
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}\|_{F}^{2}
and
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{III}}\|_{F}^{2}
in the proof of Lemma
B.9
, and the similar arguments in the proof of Lemma
B.7
, we have
\|(\widetilde{\mathcal{II}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{III}})\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}/p_{k}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1/Tp_{\text{-}k}+w_{\text{-}k}/T)
. Hence for (
B.22
), it remains to study
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}\cdot\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}/p_{k}
. To this end, from the proof of Lemma
B.9
, recall the definition
\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\ell}
and
J_{\text{-}k}=\otimes_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}J_{\ell}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{k}}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}\cdot\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k})\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k})^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}=:\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{a}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{b}+\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{c}.

Consider
\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{a}
first. Define
w_{\text{-}k}=\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}1/Tp_{\text{-}\ell}
. It then holds that

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{a}&=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\Bigg(\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}}{\sqrt{p_{\text{-}k}}}\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{\sqrt{p_{\text{-}k}}}\Bigg)E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\Bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}-\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}}{\sqrt{p_{\text{-}k}}}\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}}{\sqrt{p_{\text{-}k}}}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{k}}\|\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}\cdot\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tp}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=\mathcal{O}_{P}(w_{\text{-}k})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)\cdot\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{w_{\text{-}k}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)\Big\},\end{split}

(B.23)

where Lemma
B.4
(i) is used in the second line in which the last term appears to be exactly
\|\mathcal{IV}\|_{F}^{2}
in the proof of Lemma
B.6
, whereas the first term used
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}/p_{\text{-}k}=I_{r_{\text{-}k}}
and the same argument in (
B.20
). Similar procedures follow for
\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{b}
, by noting that
J_{\text{-}k}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
. That is, we have

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{b}&=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{w_{\text{-}k}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)\Big\}.\end{split}

(B.24)

Lastly, consider
\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{c}
. Note that Lemma
B.7
indicates
J_{\text{-}k}J_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}=I_{r_{\text{-}k}}
, and hence

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}_{c}&=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Bigg\|\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}E_{k,t}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}^{\intercal}E_{k,t}^{\intercal}\Lambda_{k}\Bigg\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\Big),\end{split}

(B.25)

where the last equality used Lemma
B.5
(iv) by equating
V=\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}
therein and
\|\ddot{\Lambda}_{\text{-}k}\|_{F}^{4}=p_{\text{-}k}^{2}
. Combining (
B.23
), (
B.24
) and (
B.25
), we hence have

\frac{1}{p_{k}}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{IV}}\cdot\Lambda_{k}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{w_{\text{-}k}\Big(\frac{1}{Tp}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)+\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\Big\}.

Thus, we may finally conclude for (
B.22
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big\|\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\Big\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\Big(\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}\ell}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{T}+\frac{1}{Tp}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)+\frac{1}{Tpp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{P}\Big\{\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}+\Big(\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}\ell}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{T}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big)\Big\}.

It remains to show the convergence result on
\widehat{H}_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}
, but given the above consistency result on
\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}
, this follows the similar arguments on
\widetilde{H}_{k}
in the proof of Lemma
B.7
and hence omitted here. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

B.3
Consistency in change point detection

B.3.1
Supporting lemmas

Let us define

\displaystyle\alpha_{T,p,k}^{2}=\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}k}}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}+\Big(\sum_{\ell\in[K]\setminus\{k\}}\frac{1}{Tp_{\text{-}\ell}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{T}+\frac{1}{p_{k}}\Big),

\displaystyle\alpha_{T,p,\text{-}k}=\sum_{w\neq k}\alpha_{T,p,w}\text{ \ and \ }\alpha_{T,p}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\alpha_{T,p,k}.

(B.26)

Lemmas on functional dependence measures.

We define the functional dependence measures of tensor-valued time series, which extend the definitions given by
60
for vector-valued time series.

Definition B.1

(Functional dependence measures)
.

Let
\{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a sequence of i.i.d. random elements with zero mean, unit variance, and uniformly bounded fourth moments. Suppose a tensor-valued time series
\{\mathcal{Y}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{K}}
, admits the causal representation
\mathcal{Y}_{t}=g_{t}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t-1},\ldots)
, where
g_{t}
is a (possibly time-varying) measurable map.
We further define the coupled process
\mathcal{Y}_{t}^{(s)}=g_{t}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t-s+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t-s}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t-s-1},\ldots)
, for
s\geq 0
,
where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t-s}^{\prime}
is an i.i.d. copy of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{0}
.
For a multi-index
\boldsymbol{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{K})\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]
, let us denote by
\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}
the corresponding entry of
\mathcal{Y}_{t}
.
Then we define the element-wise functional dependence coefficients of
\{\mathcal{Y}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
as

\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}^{(s)}\big\|_{\nu},

for some
\nu\in[2,\infty]
, and the dependence-adjusted norms (d.a.n.) as

\|\mathcal{Y}_{\cdot,\boldsymbol{i}}\|_{\nu,\beta}=\sup_{m\geq 0}\,(m+1)^{\beta}\Delta_{m,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})\text{ \ with \ }\Delta_{m,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})\!=\!\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})

for some
\beta\geq 0
.
Finally, we denote by
\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{Y})=\max_{\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]}\|\mathcal{Y}_{\cdot,\boldsymbol{i}}\|_{\nu,\beta}
the uniform d.a.n.

Lemma B.11
.

Let
\{\mathcal{Y}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a
(\nu,\beta)
-general linear tensor time series as given in Definition
3
, with
\nu>4
and
\beta>0
. For every multi-index
\boldsymbol{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{K})\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]
, write
\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{y,t,\boldsymbol{i}}
for the
\boldsymbol{i}
-th entries of
\mathcal{Y}_{t}
and
\mathcal{X}_{y,t}
, respectively.
For
\{\mathcal{Y}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
, we define

\displaystyle C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y})=\max_{\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\ldots\times[p_{K}]}\mathbb{E}(|\mathcal{X}_{y,t,\boldsymbol{i}}|^{\nu})\leq C_{\nu}<\infty,

(B.27)

with
C_{\nu}
denoted in Definition
3
(i).
Then for the uniform d.a.n. of
\{\mathcal{Y}_{t}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{Y})\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y}))^{1/\nu}\,c_{\beta}<\infty.

Proof of Lemma
B.11
.

Fix a multi–index
\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\ldots\times[p_{K}]
.
By Definition
3
, the coordinate process satisfies

\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}=\sum_{h\geq 0}a_{y,h}\mathcal{X}_{y,t-h,\boldsymbol{i}},\quad t\in\mathbb{Z}.

For some
s\geq 0
, define the coupled version
\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}^{(s)}
by replacing
\mathcal{X}_{y,t-s,\boldsymbol{i}}
with an i.i.d. copy
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{y,t-s,\boldsymbol{i}}
. Then

\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}^{(s)}=a_{y,s}\big(\mathcal{X}_{y,t-s,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{y,t-s,\boldsymbol{i}}\big).

By the triangle inequality, for some
\nu>4
, then we have

\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})=\big\|\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{Y}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}^{(s)}\big\|_{\nu}\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y}))^{1/\nu}|a_{y,s}|.

Hence, for any integer
m\geq 0
, by Definition
3
(iii),

\displaystyle\Delta_{m,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y}))^{1/\nu}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}|a_{y,s}|\leq\frac{2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}}{(m+1)^{\beta}}<\infty,\text{ \ which gives}

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Y}_{\cdot,\boldsymbol{i}}\|_{\nu,\beta}

\displaystyle=\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\Delta_{m,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{Y})\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y}))^{1/\nu}\,c_{\beta}<\infty.

Taking the maximum over
\boldsymbol{i}
, we have
\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{Y})\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{Y}))^{1/\nu}\,c_{\beta}<\infty
.
∎

Lemma B.12
.

Under Assumption
4
with
\nu>4
and
\beta\geq 0
, with
C_{\nu}(\cdot)
defined in (
B.27
) and some constant
C(\mathcal{E})>0
, we have

\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}(\mathcal{E})\leq C(\mathcal{E})\bigl(2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}_{e}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}+2(C_{\nu}(\epsilon))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}\bigr)<\infty.

Proof of Lemma
B.12
.

Fix a multi–index
\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]
and
s\geq 0
.
From Assumption
4
, for any
\boldsymbol{i}
,

\mathcal{E}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{E}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}=\bigl((\mathcal{F}_{e,t}\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{e,k})_{\boldsymbol{i}}-(\mathcal{F}_{e,t}^{(s)}\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{e,k})_{\boldsymbol{i}}\bigr)+(\Sigma_{\epsilon}\circ(\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t}^{(s)}))_{\boldsymbol{i}}.

By Minkowski’s inequality,

\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{E})=\left\|\mathcal{E}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{E}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}\right\|_{\nu}\leq\left\|\left(\big(\mathcal{F}_{e,t}-\mathcal{F}_{e,t}^{(s)}\big)\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{e,k}\right)_{\boldsymbol{i}}\right\|_{\nu}+|(\Sigma_{\epsilon})_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\epsilon).

For
\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\;\left\|\left(\big(\mathcal{F}_{e,t}-\mathcal{F}_{e,t}^{(s)}\big)\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{e,k}\right)_{\boldsymbol{i}}\right\|_{\nu}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})\prod_{k=1}^{K}\left|(A_{e,k})_{i_{k},i^{\prime}_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\left|(A_{e,k})_{i_{k},i^{\prime}_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})\prod_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i^{\prime}_{k}=1}^{r_{e,k}}\left|(A_{e,k})_{i_{k},i^{\prime}_{k}}\right|.

Since
\sum_{i^{\prime}_{k}=1}^{r_{e,k}}\left|(A_{e,k})_{i_{k},i^{\prime}_{k}}\right|\leq\|A_{e,k}\|_{\infty}
,
we see

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|\left(\big(\mathcal{F}_{e,t}-\mathcal{F}_{e,t}^{(s)}\big)\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{e,k}\right)_{\boldsymbol{i}}\right\|_{\nu}\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})\prod_{k=1}^{K}\left\|A_{e,k}\right\|_{\infty}.

Let
C(\mathcal{E})=\max\left\{\prod_{k=1}^{K}\left\|A_{e,k}\right\|_{\infty},\max_{\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\ldots\times[p_{K}]}|(\Sigma_{\epsilon})_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\right\}
, by Assumption
4
, we have
C(\mathcal{E})<\infty
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\prod_{k=1}^{K}\left\|A_{e,k}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})+\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\ldots\times[p_{K}]}|(\Sigma_{\epsilon})_{\boldsymbol{i}}|\right)\,\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{E})\,\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})+\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\epsilon)\right\}.

Applying the arguments in the proof of Lemma
B.11
, with general linear tensor series
\{\mathcal{F}_{e,t}\}
and
\{\epsilon_{t}\}
, for the given
\beta>0
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}_{e,\cdot,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}\|_{\nu,\beta}

\displaystyle=\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}_{e}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta},\text{\ and }

\displaystyle\|\epsilon_{\cdot,\boldsymbol{i}}\|_{\nu,\beta}

\displaystyle=\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\epsilon)\leq 2(C_{\nu}(\epsilon))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta},

uniformly over
\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\ldots\times[p_{K}]
and
\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]
.
Hence, the d.a.n. of
\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}_{\cdot,\boldsymbol{i}}\|_{\nu,\beta}

\displaystyle=\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{E})\left\{\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}_{e})+\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\epsilon)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{E})\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{i}^{\prime}\in[r_{e,1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{e,K}]}2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}_{e}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}+2(C_{\nu}(\epsilon))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}\right\}

\displaystyle=C(\mathcal{E})\left\{2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}_{e}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}+2(C_{\nu}(\epsilon))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}\right\}.

Taking the maximum over
\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\ldots\times[p_{K}]
, for the uniform d.a.n., we see

\displaystyle\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{E})\leq C(\mathcal{E})\bigl(2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}_{e}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}+2(C_{\nu}(\epsilon))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}\bigr)<\infty.

∎

Lemma B.13
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
(i) hold, with
\nu>4
and
\beta\geq 0
. Then for the uniform d.a.n. of
\{\mathcal{C}_{t}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{C})\leq 2c_{\lambda}^{K}r^{K}\,C(A)(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}))^{1/\nu}\,c_{\beta}<\infty,

where
C(A)
is some finite positive constant, and
C_{\nu}(\cdot)
is defined in (
B.27
).

Proof of Lemma
B.13
.

For
\mathbf{u}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{K}]
, we write
g_{t,\mathbf{u}}=(\mathcal{G}_{t})_{\mathbf{u}}
.
Recalling that
\mathcal{C}_{t}\;=\;\mathcal{G}_{t}\times_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{k}
, and fixing
\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]
, by multilinearity, each entry of
\mathcal{C}_{t}
is a finite linear combination of entries of
\mathcal{G}_{t}
,

\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots[r_{K}]}\lambda_{i_{1},u_{1}}\ldots\lambda_{i_{K},u_{K}}\ g_{t,\mathbf{u}},

For the coupled process
\{\mathcal{C}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}},s\geq 0\}
, we have

\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{C}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in[p_{1}]\times\cdots\times[p_{K}]}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots[r_{K}]}\lambda_{i_{1},u_{1}}\ldots\lambda_{i_{K},u_{K}}\big(g_{t,u_{1},\ldots,u_{K}}-g^{(s)}_{t,u_{1},\ldots,u_{K}}\big).

By triangle inequality and Assumption
2
(i),

\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{C})=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{C}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}\big\|_{\nu}\leq c_{\lambda}^{K}r^{K}\,\delta_{s,\nu}(\mathcal{G}).

Recalling that

\mathcal{G}_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\Bigl(\mathcal{F}_{t}\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{j,k}\Bigr)\,\mathbb{I}_{\{\theta_{j-1}<t\leq\theta_{j}\}},

the coupling only changes
\mathcal{F}_{t}
to
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(s)}
, so for
j\in[q+1]
, we have

\mathcal{G}_{t}-\mathcal{G}_{t}^{(s)}=\left(\mathcal{F}_{t}-\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(s)}\right)\times_{k=1}^{K}A_{j,k}.

Then for each
\mathbf{u}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{K}]
, we apply the triangle inequality to have

\displaystyle\delta_{s,\nu,\mathbf{u}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{K}]}\left|\prod_{k=1}^{K}(A_{j,k})_{u_{k},u^{\prime}_{k}}\right|\delta_{s,\nu,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})\right)\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{u^{\prime}_{k}=1}^{r_{k}}\left|(A_{j,k})_{u_{k},u^{\prime}_{k}}\right|.

Since for
j\in[q+1]
, we have

\sum_{u^{\prime}_{k}=1}^{r_{k}}\left|(A_{j,k})_{u_{k},u^{\prime}_{k}}\right|\leq\sqrt{r_{k}}\left\{\sum_{u^{\prime}_{k}=1}^{r_{k}}(A_{j,k})_{u_{k},u^{\prime}_{k}}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{r_{k}}\|A_{j,k}\|_{F},

therefore,

\delta_{s,\nu,\mathbf{u}}(\mathcal{G})\leq\left(\prod_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{r_{k}}\|A_{j,k}\|_{F}\right)\max_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{K}]}\delta_{s,\nu,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}).

By Assumption
3
(i),
we set
C(A)=\max_{j\in[q+1]}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{r_{k}}\|A_{j,k}\|_{F}<\infty
.
Applying Lemma
B.11
to the general linear tensor series
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}
gives

\|\mathcal{G}_{\cdot,\mathbf{u}}\|_{\nu,\beta}=\sup_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{\beta}\sum_{s=m}^{\infty}\delta_{s,\nu,\mathbf{u}}(\mathcal{G})\leq 2C(A)(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}<\infty,

taking the maximum over
\mathbf{u}\in[r_{1}]\times\ldots\times[r_{K}]
, we have

\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{G})\leq 2C(A)(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}<\infty.

Finally we conclude

\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{C})\leq 2c_{\lambda}^{K}r^{K}C(A)(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}<\infty.

∎

Lemma B.14
.

Under the assumptions of Lemmas
B.12
and
B.13
, the uniform d.a.n. of
\{\mathcal{X}_{t}\}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Phi_{\nu,\beta}(\mathcal{X})\leq 2c_{\lambda}^{K}r^{K}\,C(A)(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}))^{1/\nu}\,c_{\beta}+C(\mathcal{E})\bigl(2(C_{\nu}(\mathcal{F}_{e}))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}+2(C_{\nu}(\epsilon))^{1/\nu}c_{\beta}\bigr)<\infty,

where
C_{\nu}(\cdot)
is defined in (
B.27
),
C(\mathcal{E})
and
C(A)
are the same as in Lemma
B.12
and Lemma
B.13
, respectively.

Proof of Lemma
B.14
.

The conclusion follows from noting that
\mathcal{X}_{t}=\mathcal{C}_{t}+\mathcal{E}_{t}
,

\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{X})=\big\|\mathcal{X}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{X}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}\big\|_{\nu}\leq\big\|\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{C}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}\big\|_{\nu}+\big\|\mathcal{E}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}-\mathcal{E}^{(s)}_{t,\boldsymbol{i}}\big\|_{\nu}=\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{C})+\delta_{s,\nu,\boldsymbol{i}}(\mathcal{E}).

∎

Lemmas on
\mathcal{M}
-dependent processes.

To facilitate our proof, we show several results on weakly
\mathcal{M}
-dependent processes in
13
, whose definition is quoted below for completeness.

Definition B.2

(Definition 1 of
13
)
.

Let
\{Y_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a stochastic process, let
\nu\geq 1
and
\delta(m)\to 0
. We say that
\{Y_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
is weakly
\mathcal{M}
-dependent in
L^{\nu}
3
3

3

A random variable
X
is said to be in
L^{\nu}
if and only if
\mathbb{E}[|X|^{\nu}]<\infty
.
with rate function
\delta(\cdot)
if:

(i)

For any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
one can find a random variable
Y_{k}^{(m)}
with finite
\nu
-th moment such that
\|Y_{k}-Y_{k}^{(m)}\|\leq\delta(m)
.

(ii)

For any disjoint intervals
I_{1},\dots,I_{\ell}

(\ell\in\mathbb{N})
of integers and any positive integers
m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell}
, the vectors
\{Y_{j}^{(m_{1})}\}_{j\in I_{1}},\dots,\{Y_{j}^{(m_{\ell})}\}_{j\in I_{\ell}}
are independent provided
\inf\{|a-b|:a\in I_{i},b\in I_{j}\}>\max\{m_{i},m_{j}\}
for
1\leq i<j\leq\ell
.

Lemma B.15
.

(Useful results of weakly
\mathcal{M}
-dependent linear processes).

(i)
