\mathcal{T}_{\ell}>\pi_{T,p}
, where

\mathbb{L}_{s,e}=\left\{\ell\in{[|\mathbb{M}|]}:\ (a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\subseteq(s,e]\right\}.

(R2)

The estimation of
\widehat{\theta}=\tau_{\ell^{\circ}}
with
\ell^{\circ}
defined in Algorithm
1
, satisfies
\omega_{j}^{2}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\kappa_{T}^{2})
for some
j\in\mathbb{C}_{s,e}
.

At the beginning of Algorithm
1
initialized with
\widehat{\Theta}=\emptyset
, we have
(s,e)=(0,T)
meet
(S1)
–
(S2)
such that by
(R1)
–
(R2)
, we add
\widehat{\theta}
to
\widehat{\Theta}
which, for some
j\in\mathbb{C}_{0,T}=\{1,\ldots,q\}
, consistently estimates the location
\theta_{j}
. Then, we no longer have the index
j
in
\mathbb{C}_{s,e}
for the subsequently considered
(s,e)
, since either
\theta_{j}\notin\{s+1,\ldots,e-1\}
or, even so, it has been detected by either
s
or
e
such that
\min(\theta_{j}-s,e-\theta_{j})=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2})=o_{P}(\Delta_{j})
under Assumption
5
and the condition on
\kappa_{T}
in (
B.41
). Thus, once a change point is detected, its proximity to the boundary in subsequent interval splits prevents it from being detected twice, avoiding any duplicate estimator.

Once
|\widehat{\Theta}|=q
, for any
(s,e)
defined by two neighboring points in
\{0,T\}\,\cup\,\widehat{\Theta}
, we have all
\ell\in\mathbb{L}_{s,e}
satisfy
|\Theta\cap\{a_{\ell}+1,\ldots,b_{\ell}-1\}|\leq 2
. For any
\ell\in\mathbb{L}_{s,e}
satisfying
\theta_{j}\in\{a_{\ell}+1,\ldots,b_{\ell}-1\}
, we have either
\min_{\tau_{\ell}\in\{a_{\ell},b_{\ell}\}}\omega_{j}^{2}|\tau_{\ell}-\theta_{j}|=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\kappa_{T}^{2})
and
\theta_{j+1}\notin(a_{\ell},b_{\ell})
, or
\max\{\omega_{j}^{2}(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell}),\omega_{j+1}^{2}(b_{\ell}-\theta_{j+1})\}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\kappa_{T}^{2})
such that by Lemmas
B.25
–
B.27
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}=\big|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\ \mathcal{O}_{P}\big(\sqrt{\log(T)}\big)+\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}\cdot(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\alpha_{T,p}\right))

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{I}_{\{\theta_{j+1}\notin(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\}}\cdot\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}+2\,{\mathbb{I}_{\{\theta_{j+1}\in(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\}}\cdot\max_{j^{\prime}\in\{j,j+1\}}}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j^{\prime}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right\}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}_{P}\big(\sqrt{\log(T)}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{P}\big(\kappa_{T}(1+\alpha_{T,p})\big)+\mathcal{O}_{P}\big(\sqrt{\log(T)}\big)=o_{P}(\pi_{T,p}),

where the last equality follows from (
B.41
) and the conditions on
\pi_{T,p}
in Theorem
2
.
This indicates that we do not have any
T_{\ell},\,\ell\in\mathbb{L}_{s,e}
, exceed
\pi_{T,p}
, and thus the algorithm terminates.

Proof of
(R1)
.

The set
\mathbb{C}_{s,e}
is not empty by
(S1)
, then for every
j\in\mathbb{C}_{s,e}
, there exists
\ell=\ell(j)\in\mathbb{L}_{s,e}
, such that

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}/12\leq\min(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell},b_{\ell}-\theta_{j})\leq\max(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell},b_{\ell}-\theta_{j})\leq\Delta_{j}/3

(B.43)

on
\mathcal{M}_{T,M}
defined in (
B.42
).
By the construction of
(a_{\ell},b_{\ell})
, the change point
\theta_{j}
lies in the interior of the interval in the sense that
\min(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell},b_{\ell}-\theta_{j})\geq\Delta_{j}/12>\varpi_{T}
for large enough
T
, under Assumption
3
(iv).
Then from the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\geq|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}\cdot(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{6}}\sqrt{{\Delta_{j}}}\omega_{j}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)})

\displaystyle={\frac{\sqrt{{\Delta_{j}}}\omega_{j}}{2\sqrt{6}}}\cdot\left\{1+\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\alpha_{T,p}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(T)}}{\sqrt{{\Delta_{j}}}\omega_{j}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle={\frac{\sqrt{{\Delta_{j}}}\omega_{j}}{2\sqrt{6}}}\cdot(1+o_{P}(1)).

Then, from the condition on
\pi_{T,p}
in Theorem
2
, we conclude that
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}>\pi_{T,p}
.

Proof of
(R2)
.

It remains to show that
|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2})
for some
j\in\mathbb{C}_{s,e}
.
From the arguments in the proof of
(R1)
, (
B.43
) in particular, we have
b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}\leq\min_{j\in\mathbb{C}_{s,e}}2\Delta_{j}/3
, which also indicates that
|(a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}})\cap\Theta|=1
.
Let us write
(a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}]\cap\Theta=\{\theta_{j}\}
, and
\Delta^{\circ}=\min(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})
.
WLOG, we suppose that
\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}
; the case
\widehat{\theta}>\theta_{j}
is handled analogously.

First, notice that from Lemmas
B.25
–
B.27
,

\displaystyle\pi_{T,p}

\displaystyle<\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}\leq|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}+|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}\leq|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)}).

This, from the condition on
\pi_{T,p}
, implies that

\displaystyle\pi_{T,p}(1+o_{P}(1))<\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\omega_{j}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))\leq\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p})),

(B.44)

where
o_{P}
- and
\mathcal{O}_{P}
-bounds do not depend on
a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}
or
j
.

Step 1.
We first establish that
\theta_{j}
remains after trimming of the boundaries by
\varpi_{T}
in the maximization defining
\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}
.
If this is not the case, we can find another interval, say
(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\in\mathbb{M}
, such that
b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}=b_{\ell}-a_{\ell}
and
(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\cap\Theta=\{\theta_{j}\}
.
Further, noting that
\min(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})\leq\varpi_{T}
, we have
\min(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell},b_{\ell}-\theta_{j})\geq(b_{\ell}-a_{\ell})/2-\varpi_{T}>\varpi_{T}
, from the construction of
\mathbb{M}
and the condition on
\varpi_{T}
.
From this, we derive that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}=\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell})(b_{\ell}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell}-a_{\ell}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\right)\omega_{j}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\left(\sqrt{\frac{((b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})/2-\varpi_{T})((b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})/2+\varpi_{T})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\varpi_{T}(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}-\varpi_{T})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\right)\omega_{j}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\left(\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})/2-\varpi_{T}}{2}}-\sqrt{\varpi_{T}}\right)\omega_{j}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))\gtrsim_{P}\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j},

(B.45)

thanks to Lemma
B.27
for small enough
c_{\varpi}
, where the
\mathcal{O}_{P}
-bound depends on a quantity that does not vary with
a_{\ell},b_{\ell},a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}
or
j
.
Then, recalling that
\tau_{\ell}=\argmax_{a_{\ell}+\varpi_{T}<\tau<b_{\ell}-\varpi_{T}}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\tau,b_{\ell}}|_{2}
, it follows that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\tau_{\ell},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}\geq|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}-\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-\left(|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}+|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}-\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell},\theta_{j},b_{\ell}}|_{2}-\left(|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}+|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{P}

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)}),

from Lemma
B.26
and (
B.45
), indicating that
\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}<\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
due to (
B.44
).
This contradicts the definition of
\ell^{\circ}
in Algorithm
1
, i.e. we have
\min(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})>\varpi_{T}
.
Also, together with (
B.41
), we have neither
a_{\ell^{\circ}}
or
b_{\ell^{\circ}}
estimate
\theta_{j}
in the sense that the following does not hold:
\min_{\tau\in\{a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}}\}}\omega_{j}^{2}|\tau-\theta_{j}|=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\kappa_{T}^{2})
.

Step 2.
Next, we show that

\big|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}\big|\leq\frac{1}{4}\Delta^{\circ}.

(B.46)

If not, by Lemma
B.27
and Lemma S3.5 in
22
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}-\big|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{1-|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|/(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{1+|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|/(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}}\right)\omega_{j}\cdot(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\omega_{j}\cdot(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))\gtrsim\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j}(1+o_{P}(1)),

(B.47)

On the other hand, since
\theta_{j}
is admissible from Step 1, we see from that
\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}=\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j}b_{\ell^{\circ}}}
by construction, and that
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\leq\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}
by Lemma
B.25
.
Then from Lemma
B.26
,

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}-\big|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\,\big|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}+\big|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}_{P}\big(\sqrt{\log(T)}\big)=\,{o_{P}(\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j})},

where the last equation follows from (
B.44
).
This contradicts (
B.47
).

Step 3.
We show that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq 0,\text{ \ where \ }\mathbf{g}^{\circ}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}}{\big|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\big|_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

with
|\mathbf{g}^{\circ}|_{2}=1
(recall that
d=\sum_{k\in[K]}r_{k}(r_{k}+1)/2
). It trivially holds that

\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}=\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}=(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}>0,

while for
(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}
, we have
(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\leq|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}
.
By construction,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}-\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}+{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right)-(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\left({\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right)

\displaystyle=:U_{1}-U_{2}.

First, for
U_{2}
, let us write

\displaystyle-U_{2}

\displaystyle=-(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\left({\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left(\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\sum_{t=\widehat{\theta}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}V_{t}-\frac{1}{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\widehat{\theta}}(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}V_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left(\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}V_{t}-\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\theta_{j}}(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}V_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\left[\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\sum_{t=\widehat{\theta}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}V_{t}-\frac{1}{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\widehat{\theta}}V_{t}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left.\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\left(\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}V_{t}-\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\theta_{j}}V_{t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\left(\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}V_{t}-\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\theta_{j}}V_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=:U_{21}-U_{22}.

Firstly, note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\!\sum_{t=\widehat{\theta}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\!V^{(k)}_{t}-\frac{1}{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\!\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\widehat{\theta}}\!V^{(k)}_{t}-\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\left(\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\!\sum_{t=\theta_{j}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\!V^{(k)}_{t}-\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\!\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\theta_{j}}\!V^{(k)}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{vech}\left\{\!\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\!\left(\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\Gamma_{G,(j)}^{(k)}+\frac{\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\Gamma_{G,(j-1)}^{(k)}-\!\Gamma_{G,(j-1)}^{(k)}\!-\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\Gamma_{G,(j)}^{(k)}+\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\Gamma_{G,(j-1)}^{(k)}\right)\!\widehat{H}_{k}\!\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,0,

for all
k\in[K]
, which gives
U_{21}=0
.
By Lemma
B.26
, we have
|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)})
.
Recall that by Lemma
B.27
, we have
|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}=\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\cdot(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))
, where
\mathcal{O}_{P}
-bound holds uniformly over all the triplets of the indices in consideration, which gives

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}\leq\left|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}.

Then, by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{\intercal}\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle=\left|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right)^{\intercal}\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle\geq\left|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}^{2}-\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}\left|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}\left(\left|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}-\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}\right)\geq 0,

which implies

\displaystyle(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}=\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{\intercal}}{\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq 0.

Step 4.
We are now ready to derive the refined rate of estimation.
From Lemma
B.25
, we know that
|\mathcal{V}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\tau,b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|_{2}
attains its maximum at
\tau=\theta_{j}
within
(a_{\ell^{\circ}},b_{\ell^{\circ}})
.
By similar arguments,
|(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\tau,b_{\ell^{\circ}}}|
attains its maximum at
\tau=\theta_{j}
. Therefore, we have
(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq 0
.
Then together with Lemma
B.26
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}=(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\leq(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)})\leq(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)}).

On the other hand, due to the construction of
\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\circ}}
, again by using Lemma
B.26
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}\geq\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)}).

Combining the above, by Assumption
5
and Lemma
B.27
, we see

\displaystyle(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left|{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right|_{2}+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{*}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}))+\mathcal{O}_{P}(\sqrt{\log(T)})

(Lemma
B.27
)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\omega_{j}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(1+\alpha_{T,p}+{\frac{\sqrt{\log(T)}}{\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\omega_{j}\cdot(1+o_{P}(1)).

(from (
B.44
))

Then for
U_{22}
, by Lemma 7 of
56
,

\displaystyle U_{22}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot(\mathbf{g}^{\circ})^{\intercal}\left(\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}V_{t}-\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\theta_{j}}V_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\right)^{-1}}\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\omega_{j}\cdot(1+o_{P}(1))

\displaystyle=\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\right\}\,\omega_{j}\cdot(1+o_{P}(1))

(B.48)

\displaystyle\geq\,\frac{2\big|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}\big|}{3\sqrt{6}\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\;\omega_{j}\cdot(1+o_{P}(1)).

Altogether, we have

\displaystyle U_{2}\geq\frac{2\big|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}\big|}{3\sqrt{6}\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\;\omega_{j}\cdot(1+o_{P}(1)).

(B.49)

As for
U_{1}
, by Lemmas
B.16
(ii) and
B.18
, and Equation (C.9) in
22
, we have

\displaystyle|U_{1}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left\{\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\sum_{t=\widehat{\theta}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}(\widehat{V}_{t}-V_{t})-\frac{1}{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\widehat{\theta}}(\widehat{V}_{t}-V_{t})\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left.\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left\{\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\sum_{t=\theta_{j}+1}^{b_{\ell^{\circ}}}(\widehat{V}_{t}-V_{t})-\frac{1}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}\sum_{t=a_{\ell^{\circ}}+1}^{\theta_{j}}(\widehat{V}_{t}-V_{t})\right\}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left({\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta}}^{(1)}+{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta}}^{(2)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left.\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left({\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}+{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\right)\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\cdot\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}-\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\right||{\Omega_{j}}|_{2}H_{1}H_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left|\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}}\right|\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left|\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\right|\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta}}{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta}}{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}}\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}-\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\right|\omega_{j}H_{1}H_{3}

\displaystyle=:U_{11}+U_{12}+U_{13}+U_{14}+U_{15}.

(B.50)

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\tau,b_{\ell^{\circ}}}=\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\tau,b_{\ell^{\circ}}}-\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j}\,b_{\ell^{\circ}}}
, then
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}=0
. Since
\widehat{\theta}=\argmax_{a_{\ell^{\circ}}\leq\tau\leq b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\mathcal{T}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\tau,b_{\ell^{\circ}}}<\theta_{j}
by construction and from (
B.46
), it follows that for some fixed constant
C>0
and
\kappa_{T}\to\infty
arbitrarily slow,

\displaystyle\quad\;\left\{\omega_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}})\leq-{C}\kappa_{T}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq\max_{\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}+\Delta^{\circ}/4}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq 0\right\}{\subset\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}|U_{1}|-U_{2}\geq 0\right\}.}

Then from (
B.49
) and (
B.50
),

\displaystyle\quad\;\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}{|U_{1}|}-{U_{2}}\geq 0\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\frac{U_{11}+U_{12}+U_{13}+U_{14}+U_{15}}{U_{2}}\geq 1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\frac{U_{11}}{U_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{5}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\frac{U_{12}}{U_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{5}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\frac{U_{13}}{U_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{5}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\frac{U_{14}}{U_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{5}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\frac{U_{15}}{U_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{5}\right\}=:W_{1}+W_{2}+W_{3}+W_{4}+W_{5}.

Firstly, for
W_{1}
, by Lemma S3.5 of
22
, and by (
B.46
),

\displaystyle U_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left|\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}}\right|\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\left|\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}}\frac{(\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}\right|\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}}\cdot|\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta}|\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\cdot\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle\geq{\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\circ}}{2}}},

(B.51)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle\geq{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(B.52)

Then, by Assumption
5
, Lemmas
B.16
(ii),
B.20
, and
B.21
, and (
B.49
),

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}+\Delta^{\circ}/4}\frac{U_{11}}{U_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{5},\,{\mathcal{H}}_{T,p}^{c}\right\}+\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{T,p})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{1}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\cdot\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}\geq\frac{3\sqrt{6}\omega_{j}}{10\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}{(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})}}\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\geq\frac{3\sqrt{6}\omega_{j}}{10\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}+o(1)

(Lemma
B.16
(ii))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\sqrt{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}\geq{\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{10}\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}\omega_{j}}\cdot\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}

(from (
B.51
))

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left\{\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\theta_{j},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\geq{\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{10}\omega_{j}}\cdot\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}+o(1)

(from (
B.52
))

\displaystyle=o(1).

(Lemmas
B.20
,
B.21
and (
B.44
))

We handle
W_{2}
analogously.
As for
W_{3}
, by (
B.46
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}{(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}}\leq{\sqrt{\frac{4}{3\Delta^{\circ}}}},

which gives

\displaystyle U_{13}\leq{\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}}\frac{|\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta}|}{\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right).

Then by Assumption
5
, Lemmas
B.16
(ii),
B.24
and (
B.49
), with
\kappa_{T}
satisfying (
B.41
), we have

\displaystyle W_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}{\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\left(\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}+\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\right)\geq\frac{3\sqrt{6}\omega_{j}}{10\sqrt{\Delta^{\circ}}}\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{T,p})

(Lemma
B.16
(ii))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\sqrt{\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(1)}\big|_{2}\geq{\frac{9\sqrt{2}}{40}}\kappa_{T}\cdot\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\big|{\mathcal{J}}_{\widehat{\theta},\theta_{j}}^{(2)}\big|_{2}\geq{\frac{9\sqrt{2}}{40}}\omega_{j}\cdot\big(1+C_{H}\alpha_{T,p}\big)\right\}}+o(1)

(Lemma
B.16
(ii))

\displaystyle=o(1).

(Lemma
B.24
and Assumption
5
)

We can handle
W_{4}
analogously.
Finally, for
W_{5}
, noting that

\displaystyle U_{15}

\displaystyle=\left|\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j})(\theta_{j}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}-\sqrt{\frac{(b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}-a_{\ell^{\circ}})}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-a_{\ell^{\circ}}}}\frac{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\theta_{j}}{b_{\ell^{\circ}}-\widehat{\theta}}\right|\omega_{j}\cdot H_{1}H_{3},

we invoke (
B.48
) and the arguments used in deriving (
B.49
) to have

\displaystyle W_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}H_{1}H_{3}\geq\frac{1}{5}(1+o(1))\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{S}^{(1)}_{T,p}\cup\mathcal{S}^{(2)}_{T,p}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{(1)}_{T,p}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{(2)}_{T,p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{T,p})+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{S}^{(1)}_{T,p}\cup\mathcal{S}^{(2)}_{T,p}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{(1)}_{T,p}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{(2)}_{T,p}\right)=o(1),

by Lemma
B.16
(ii).
Combining the bounds on
W_{1}
–
W_{5}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\omega_{j}^{2}(\theta_{j}-\widehat{\theta})\leq-C\kappa_{T}^{2}\right\}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\theta_{j}-\Delta^{\circ}/4+1\leq\widehat{\theta}\leq\theta_{j}-C\omega_{j}^{-2}\kappa_{T}^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{a_{\ell^{\circ}},\widehat{\theta},b_{\ell^{\circ}}}\geq 0\right\}=o(1).

Analogous arguments apply to the case where
\widehat{\theta}>\theta_{j}
. Hence, concluding
(R1)
–
(R2)
, the proof is complete.

B.4
Mode-identification

B.4.1
Supporting lemmas

Let us define for
j\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Theta_{j}^{-}&=\left\{a_{\ell}:\,(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\in\mathbb{M}^{*},\,b_{\ell}\in[\theta_{j}-3T/2^{\mu_{T}+1},\theta_{j}+T/2^{\mu_{T}}]\right\},\\
\Theta_{j}^{+}&=\left\{b_{\ell}:\,(a_{\ell},b_{\ell}]\in\mathbb{M}^{*},\,a_{\ell}\in[\theta_{j}-T/2^{\mu_{T}},\theta_{j}+3T/2^{\mu_{T}+1}]\right\},\end{split}

(B.53)

with
\Theta_{0}^{\pm}=\{0\}
and
\Theta_{q+1}^{\pm}=\{T\}
.
By construction of
\mathbb{M}^{*}
, all the intervals therein have length
T/2^{\mu_{T}}
such that under Assumption
3
(iv), for
T
large enough,

(a)

|\Theta_{j}^{\pm}|\leq 4
for all
j
,

(b)

for any
a\in\Theta_{j}^{-}
(resp.
b\in\Theta_{j}^{+}
), we have
a\in(\theta_{j-1},\theta_{j}]
(resp.
b\in[\theta_{j},\theta_{j+1})
), and

(c)

for any
j\in[q+1]
,
a\in\Theta^{+}_{j-1}
and
b\in\Theta^{-}_{j}
, we have
b-a\geq c_{\Delta}T-5T/2^{\mu_{T}}\geq c_{\Delta}T/2
.

Lemma B.28
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
hold, with
\nu>8
and
\beta\geq 0
.

(i)

For any
a<b
and
c<d
, with the the definitions in (
3.2
) and the notations in Lemma
B.18
, we have

\displaystyle\;\quad\left\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a,b}^{(k)}-\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,c,d}^{(k)}-\left(\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}{\Gamma}_{G,a,b}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}{\Gamma}_{G,c,d}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\Big\|{\mathcal{J}}_{k,a,b}^{(1)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{k,c,d}^{(1)}\Big\|_{F}+2\,\Big\|{\mathcal{J}}_{k,a,b}^{(2)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{k,c,d}^{(2)}\Big\|_{F}

\displaystyle\;\quad+4\sqrt{2}\cdot\|\widehat{H}_{k}\|\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{k}}}\left(\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\right)\right\|_{F}\cdot\left|\mathrm{vech}\left(\Gamma_{G,a,b}^{(k)}-\Gamma_{G,c,d}^{(k)}\right)\right|_{2}.

(ii)

We have

\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{+}\\
b_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{-}\end{subarray}}\max_{k\in[K]}\left\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\!\!-\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\!\!-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\left({\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\!\!-{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\right)\widehat{H}_{k}\right\|=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{1}{p}\right).

Proof of Lemma
B.28
.

Part (i) follows directly from carefully checking the proof of Lemma
B.18
.

For part (ii), fix any
k\in[K]
.
By
(b)
–
(c)
, we have all
\Gamma^{(k)}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}
for
a_{j}\in\Theta^{+}_{j}
and
b_{j+1}\in\Theta^{-}_{j+1}
well-defined and

\Gamma^{(k)}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}=\Gamma^{(k)}_{G,j+1}
, for all
j=0,\ldots,q
.

Note that
\|X\|_{F}\leq 2\cdot|\mathrm{vech}(X)|_{2}
for any symmetric matrix
X
.
By part (i), we have

\displaystyle\;\quad\left\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}-\left(\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\Big\|{\mathcal{J}}_{k,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(1)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{k,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(1)}\Big\|_{F}+2\,\Big\|{\mathcal{J}}_{k,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(2)}-{\mathcal{J}}_{k,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(2)}\Big\|_{F}

\displaystyle\;\quad+4\sqrt{2}\cdot\|\widehat{H}_{k}\|\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{k}}}\left(\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\right)\right\|_{F}\cdot\left|\mathrm{vech}\left(\Gamma_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\Gamma_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\right)\right|_{2}.

(B.54)

Following the arguments in the proof of Lemmas
B.19
and
B.20
, while noticing that by
(a)
, there are only finitely many tuples of
(a_{j},b_{j+1})
in consideration, and that
b_{j+1}-a_{j}\geq c_{\Delta}T/2
by
(c)
, we can analogously show that as
\min(T,p_{1},\ldots,p_{K})\to\infty
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{0\leq j\leq q}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{+}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{-}}\max_{k\in[K]}\sqrt{b_{j+1}-a_{j}}\Big|{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(1)}\Big|_{2}\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\right\}\to 0,

(B.55)

for some finite constant
C_{1}^{\prime}>0
.
Similarly, we have from the proof of Lemma
B.21
that as
\min(T,p_{1},\ldots,p_{K})\to\infty
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{0\leq j\leq q}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{+}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{-}}\max_{k\in[K]}\Big|{\mathcal{J}}_{a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(2)}\Big|_{2}\geq C_{2}^{\prime}\alpha_{T,p}\right\}\to 0,

(B.56)

for some finite constant
C_{2}^{\prime}>0
, and
\alpha_{T,p}
is defined in (
B.26
).
Further note that by Assumptions
1
and
3
, Lemma
B.10
and (
B.26
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{k\in[K]}\|\widehat{H}_{k}\|\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{k}}}\left(\widehat{\Lambda}_{k}-\Lambda_{k}\widehat{H}_{k}\right)\right\|_{F}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{+}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta_{j}^{-}}\left|\mathrm{vech}\left(\Gamma_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\Gamma_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\right)\right|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(\alpha_{T,p}).

(B.57)

Altogether, we conclude the proof of part (ii) by
combining (
B.54
), (
B.55
), (
B.56
) and (
B.57
) with
(c)
.
∎

Lemma B.29
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
hold, with
\nu>8
and
\beta\geq 0
.
For each
j\in[q]
, we define

\displaystyle\widehat{\Xi}^{(k)}_{a_{j-1},b_{j},a_{j},b_{j+1}}=\textnormal{\text{tr}}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)})\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\textnormal{\text{tr}}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)})\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta^{+}_{j}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta^{-}_{j}}\max_{k\in[K]}\left\|\widehat{\Xi}^{(k)}_{a_{j-1},b_{j},a_{j},b_{j+1}}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\right\|=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{1}{p}\right).

Proof of Lemma
B.29
.

As an auxiliary result, for any symmetric and positive semi-definite matrices
B
and
C
of fixed dimensions and with
\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B),\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C)\asymp_{P}1
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{B}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B)}-\frac{C}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C)}\right\|=\left\|\frac{B-C}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B)}+C\left(\frac{1}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B)}-\frac{1}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C)}\right)\right\|\lesssim_{P}\|B-C\|.

Similarly, for any symmetric and positive semi-definite matrices
B_{1}
,
B_{2}
,
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
of fixed dimensions and with
\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{1})\asymp_{P}\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{2})\asymp_{P}1
and
\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C_{1})
and
\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C_{2})
are both positive with probability approaching one:

\displaystyle\;\quad\left\|\frac{B_{1}}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{1})}-\frac{C_{1}}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C_{1})}+\frac{B_{2}}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{2})}-\frac{C_{2}}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C_{2})}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{B_{1}-C_{1}}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{1})}+C_{1}\left(\frac{1}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{1})}-\frac{1}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C_{1})}\right)+\frac{B_{2}-C_{2}}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{2})}+C_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(B_{2})}-\frac{1}{\textnormal{\text{tr}}(C_{2})}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{P}\|B_{1}-C_{1}+B_{2}-C_{2}\|.

(B.58)

Next, fix any
k\in[K]
.
Notice that by
(b)
–
(c)
, Definition
1
, (
2.3
) and (
3.2
), we have, for all
j\in[q+1]
,
a_{j}\in\Theta^{-}_{j}
and
b_{j}\in\Theta^{+}_{j}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{G,(j)}^{(k)}=\mathbb{E}\Big\{\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{\theta_{j}})\textnormal{{mat}}_{k}(\mathcal{G}_{\theta_{j}})^{\intercal}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big\{\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)^{\intercal}\Big(\otimes_{i=K,i\neq k}^{1}A_{j,i}\Big)\Big\}\cdot A_{j,k}A_{j,k}^{\intercal}.

Hence from (
3.5
), we may write, for
j\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\Xi_{j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big({\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\Big)^{-1}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big({\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\Big)^{-1}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\;\quad\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta^{+}_{j}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta^{-}_{j}}\max_{k\in[K]}\left\|\widehat{\Xi}^{(k)}_{a_{j-1},b_{j},a_{j},b_{j+1}}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\right\|

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta^{+}_{j}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta^{-}_{j}}\max_{k\in[K]}\Bigg\|\left\{\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big(\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\Big)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big(\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\Big)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\right\}

\displaystyle\;\quad-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\left\{\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big({\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\Big)^{-1}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\textnormal{\text{tr}}\Big({\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\Big)^{-1}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\right\}\widehat{H}_{k}\Bigg\|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{P}\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{a_{j}\in\Theta^{+}_{j}}\max_{b_{j}\in\Theta^{-}_{j}}\max_{k\in[K]}\left\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}-\widehat{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}-\left(\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j},b_{j+1}}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}{\Gamma}_{G,a_{j-1},b_{j}}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{1}{p}\right),

where we make use of (
B.58
), Assumption
3
and the fact that
\widehat{H}_{k}
is asymptotically orthogonal according to Lemma
B.10
, for the first inequality, and the last equality follows from Lemma
B.28
(ii), which completes the proof.
∎

B.4.2
Proof of Theorem
3

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Throughout this proof, we use that for
T
large enough, we have

\upsilon_{T}=\kappa_{T}^{2}\leq\min_{j\in[q]}\omega_{j}^{2}\Delta_{j}/2^{\mu_{T}}
, which is in line with (
B.41
).
By Theorem
2
, we have

\displaystyle\;\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{q}=q,\,\max_{j\in[q]}\,|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|\leq\frac{T}{2^{\mu_{T}}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{q}=q,\,\max_{j\in[q]}\,\omega_{j}^{2}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|\leq\min_{j\in[q]}\omega_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{T}{2^{\mu_{T}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{q}=q,\,\max_{j\in[q]}\,\omega_{j}^{2}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{j}|\leq\kappa_{T}^{2}\right)\to 1

as
\min(T,p_{1},\ldots,p_{K})\to\infty
.
Then, it follows that on the event
\{\widehat{q}=q,\,\max_{j\in[q]}\omega_{j}^{2}|\widehat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}|\leq\kappa_{T}^{2}\}
, we have

\widehat{\theta}_{j}^{-1}\in\Theta_{j}^{-1}
and
\widehat{\theta}_{j}^{+}\in\Theta_{j}^{+}
for all
j\in[q]
, by their construction in (
3.6
) and (
B.53
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\;\quad\frac{\zeta_{T,p}}{\min_{j\in[q]}\min_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{j}}\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}\|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\zeta_{T,p}}{\min_{j\in[q]}\min_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{j}}\|\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\|-\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{j}}\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\|}

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta_{T,p}}{\min_{j\in[q]}\min_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{j}}\|\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\|+\mathcal{O}_{P}(T^{-1/2}+p^{-1})}

\displaystyle\asymp_{P}\frac{\zeta_{T,p}}{\min_{j\in[q]}\min_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{j}}\|\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\|}=o(1),

where the first equality holds thanks to Lemma
B.29
, the second under Assumption
7
, and the final under the condition imposed on
\zeta_{T,p}
.
Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{k\in[K]\setminus{\mathcal{K}}_{j}}\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[q]}\max_{k\in[K]}\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}-\widehat{H}_{k}^{\intercal}\Xi_{j}^{(k)}\widehat{H}_{k}\|=o_{P}(\zeta_{T,p}),

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Numerical experiments for threshold selection

C.1
Threshold
\pi_{T,p}
for change point detection

We simulate tensor time series under the null model
(S0)
with no change points in Appendix
D.1
.
Varying the dimensions and core factor tensor ranks as

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\in\{400,1200,2000,2800,3200,4000,4800,5600\},

\displaystyle(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle\in\{(10,10,10),(10,10,100),(10,20,40),(20,20,20)\},

\displaystyle(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})

\displaystyle\in\{(2,2,2),(2,3,2),(2,3,3),(3,3,3)\},

while fixing
\rho_{f}=0.7
, we generate
100
realizations for each combination.
Next, for each realization, we take
\max_{(s,e]\in\mathbb{M}}\max_{s<\tau<e}\mathcal{T}_{s,\tau,e}
, the maximum detector statistic over the collection of seeded intervals, and record the
0.9
-empirical quantiles of these maxima for each setting.
Then, we regress them (contained in the
R
object
y
) onto the corresponding on functions of
T
and
d=\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}(r_{k}+1)/2
, whose choices are motivated by the approximation of the asymptotic null distribution made in
21
,
using the
R
function
lm
:

lm(y ~ sqrt(d) + sqrt(log(T)) + log(log(T))/sqrt(log(T)) + 1/sqrt(log(T)))

The fitted model has
R^{2}_{\text{adj}}=0.9397
, and Figure
C.1
displays diagnostic plots which show that the model provides a good fit to the data.
As the model depends only on
T
and
d
, we use it for determining the threshold
\pi_{T,p}
.

Figure C.1:
Diagnostic plots for the model regressing the empirical 0.9-quantiles of the maximal detector statistics on the functions of
T
and
d
.
Empirical 0.9-quantiles vs. the fitted values (left), residuals vs. the fitted values (middle), and the normal Q-Q plot of the standardized residuals.

C.2
Threshold
\zeta_{T,p}
for mode-identification

We calibrate the threshold for mode-identification through numerical experiments using the true change point locations; see Figures
C.2
–
C.3
. Specifically, under
(S1)
and
(S2)
, we examine the values of
\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}/(T^{-1/2}+p^{-1})
computed with the knowledge of the true change point locations (i.e. with
\widehat{\theta}^{\pm}_{j}=\theta_{j}
), over varying
T
and
(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})
. The empirical
0.99
-quantile of the pooled statistics corresponding to the modes for which the changes are not mode-identifiable, is approximately
3.47
under
(S1)
and
3.55
under
(S2)
.
This motivates use to set
\zeta_{T,p}=3.5(T^{-1/2}+p^{-1})
and declare mode
k
to be identified whenever
\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}\|\geq\zeta_{T,p}
.

Figure C.2:
(S1)
Pooled mode-identification results using the true change point locations. For each true change point and each mode, the quantity
\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}\|/(T^{-1/2}+p^{-1})
is plotted after pooling across all three change points, sample sizes
T\in\{400,800,1600,3200\}
, and tensor dimensions
(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\in\{(10,10,10),(10,10,100),(10,20,40),(20,20,20)\}
. Points are colored according to whether the corresponding mode
k
is mode-identifiable at the change point. The dashed horizontal line denotes the empirical
0.99
-quantile of the pooled statistics from the modes for which the change is not mode-identifiable.

Figure C.3:
(S2)
Pooled mode-identification results using the true change point locations. For each true change point and each mode, the quantity
\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k)}\|/(T^{-1/2}+p^{-1})
is plotted after pooling across all three change points, sample sizes
T\in\{400,800,1600,3200\}
, and tensor dimensions
(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\in\{(10,10,10),(10,10,100),(10,20,40),(20,20,20)\}
. Points are colored according to whether the corresponding mode
k
is mode-identifiable at the change point. The dashed horizontal line denotes the empirical
0.99
-quantile of the pooled statistics from the modes for which the change is not mode-identifiable.

Appendix D
Complete numerical experiments

D.1
Simulation scenarios

Throughout, we consider order-
3
tensor time series
\{\mathcal{X}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
generated under (
2.1
)–(
2.2
) with
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})=(3,3,3)
.
Also,
\Lambda_{1,k},\,k\in[3]
, have their entries generated independently from the uniform distribution
\mathcal{U}(-1,1)
.
The factor process is generated as

\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{F}_{t})=\rho_{f}\,\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})+\varepsilon_{t}
with
\varepsilon_{t}\mathrel{{\mathop{\sim}\limits}}\mathcal{N}_{r}({0},\,(1-\rho_{f}^{2})I_{r})
,
and the idiosyncratic tensor
\mathcal{E}_{t}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries, with
\rho_{f}\in\{0,0.7\}
in scenarios
(S0)
–
(S2)
below, while we focus on the case where
\rho_{f}=0
under
(S3)
.

(S0)

Null scenario.

We consider the tensor factor model as in (
2.1
) with
\Theta=\emptyset
, i.e. the loading matrix is time-invariant.

(S1)

Single-mode change at each change point.

Extending the data generating processes from
25
and
11
to the tensor setting, we consider order-
3
tensor time series with
q=3
change points and the following transformation matrices (see (
2.2
)):

A_{1,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0.5&0&0\\
a_{1,21}&1&0\\
a_{1,31}&a_{1,32}&1.5\end{bmatrix},\quad A_{2,2}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&0\end{bmatrix},\text{\ and\ }A_{3,3}=\begin{bmatrix}a_{3,11}&a_{3,12}&a_{3,13}\\
a_{3,21}&a_{3,22}&a_{3,23}\\
a_{3,31}&a_{3,32}&a_{3,33}\end{bmatrix},

where
a_{1,\ell h}\mathrel{{\mathop{\sim}\limits}}\mathcal{N}(0,\,1)
for
(\ell,h)\in\{(2,1),(3,1),(3,2)\}
and
a_{3,\ell h}\mathrel{{\mathop{\sim}\limits}}\mathcal{N}(0,\,1/3)
for
\ell,h\in[3]
. Then at each
j\in[3]
, we set
\Lambda_{j+1,k}=\Lambda_{j,k}A_{j,k}
for
k=j
and
\Lambda_{j+1,k}=\Lambda_{j,k},\,k\neq j
, i.e.
{\mathcal{K}}_{j}=\{j\}
for all
j\in[3]
.
We present the results when
\Theta=\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.625T\rfloor\}
with serial correlation
\rho_{f}=0.7
in the main text (Section
5.2
).

(S2)

Multi-mode change at each change point.

We modify the scenario in
(S1)
by allowing multiple modes to change at
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{3}
, including mode-(un)identifiable ones. For this, we additionally set
\Lambda_{2,3}=\Lambda_{1,3}\cdot 3I_{3}
and
\Lambda_{4,2}=\Lambda_{3,2}A_{3,2}
with
A_{3,2}=\mathrm{diag}(1,0.6,0.2)
, while all other loading matrices are generated as in
(S1)
.
Then, we have
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}=\{1\},\,{\mathcal{K}}_{2}=\{2\}
and
{\mathcal{K}}_{3}=\{2,3\}
.

(S3)

Single change point detection.

To ease the investigation of the downstream task of mode-wise loading space estimation after change point detection and mode-identification in Appendix
D.4
, we consider the situation where there is a single change point. We apply the same rank-deficient transformation matrix as in
(S1)
to the first mode only, so that
\Lambda_{2,1}=\Lambda_{1,1}A_{2,2}
while
\Lambda_{2,k}=\Lambda_{1,k}I_{3}
for
k\in\{2,3\}
, with
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}=\{1\}
.

With varying
T\in\{400,800,1600,3200\}
, for
(S0)
–
(S2)
, we consider dimensions

(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\in\{(10,10,10),(10,10,100),(10,20,40),(20,20,20)\},

while for
(S3)
, we consider

(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\in\{(10,10,10),(10,10,100),(10,100,10),(100,10,10)\}.

We consider both the cases when the change points are equally (
\Theta=\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.75T\rfloor\}
) and unequally (
\Theta=\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.625T\rfloor\}
) spaced in
(S1)
and
(S2)
.
In
(S3)
, we set
\Theta=\{\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor\}
.

Throughout, we generate
N=100
realizations for each setting.

D.2
Results for change point detection

For each
n\in[N]
, denote by
\widehat{q}^{(n)}
and
\widehat{\theta}_{j}^{(n)}
the number of change point estimators and their locations in the
n
-th replication. We report the distribution of
\widehat{q}^{(n)}-q
, as well as the accuracy in estimating each
\theta_{j}
measured as the proportion of replications over which it has an estimator within the distance
2\log(T)
, namely

\displaystyle\text{Accuracy}_{j}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{I}\left\{\min_{1\leq\ell\leq\widehat{q}^{(n)}}\Big|\widehat{\theta}_{\ell}^{(n)}-\theta_{j}\Big|\leq 2\log(T)\right\},

following
44
. This criterion is defined point-wise for each true change point and does not rely on the estimated number of change points being the same as the true number.
We additionally report the accuracy of the change point estimators conditional on the successful detection of all three change points, by considering the realizations where
\widehat{q}^{(n)}=\widehat{q}
, and
\widehat{\theta}^{(n)}_{j}\in((\theta_{j-1}+\theta_{j})/2,(\theta_{j}+\theta_{j+1})/2]
for all
j\in[q]
; we denote the index set of those realizations by
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}\subset[N]
.
We also report the runtime in the format of “average
\pm
standard deviation” over
100
realizations per configuration.
As in Section
5
, we report the results from applying the binary segmentation procedure of
3
, referred to as
“LR”
, which recursively performs likelihood-ratio test, to the vectorized time series data.

D.2.1
(S0)
Null scenarios

Tables
D.1
–
D.4
report the summary of (spuriously) detected change points under the null scenario where there is no change point.
We observe that while its performance is influenced by the degree of serial correlations, generally TFMseg returns few false positives across all the settings in consideration.

D.2.3
(S1)
Single-mode change with unequally spaced change points

We consider when the change points are unequally spaced with
\Theta=\big\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.625T\rfloor\big\}
under
(S1)
, see Tables
D.13
–
D.16
and Figure
1
for the case of serially dependent data with
\rho_{f}=0.7
, and Tables
D.17
–
D.20
and Figure
D.4
for the case when the data are serially uncorrelated.

D.2.4
(S2)
Multi-mode change with equal-spaced change points

We consider when the change points are equally spaced with
\Theta=\big\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.75T\rfloor\big\}
under
(S2)
, see Tables
D.21
–
D.24
together with Figure
D.5
for the case of serially dependent data with
\rho_{f}=0.7
, and Tables
D.25
–
D.28
and Figure
D.6
for the case when the data are serially uncorrelated.
Comparing the results to those obtained under
(S1)
, we note that the increased size of change as measured by
\omega_{j}
in Definition
1
, thanks to the additional mode undergoing a shift under
(S2)
, benefits TFMseg and leads to its improved performance; this is not the case for LR as vectorization of the data does not preserve the increased change size.

D.3
Results for mode-identification

Recalling the definition of
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}
in Appendix
D.2
, we report the following performance metrics:
With
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{j}^{(n)}=\{k\in[K]:\,\|\widehat{\Xi}_{j}^{(k),(n)}\|>\zeta_{T,p}\}
,

\text{TPR}_{j}=\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}|}\sum_{n\in\widehat{\mathcal{D}}}\frac{|\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}}_{j}^{(n)}\cap{\mathcal{K}}_{j}|}{\max(|{\mathcal{K}}_{j}|,\,1)}\text{ \ and \ }\text{FPR}_{j}=\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}|}\sum_{n\in\widehat{\mathcal{D}}}\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{j}^{(n)}\setminus{\mathcal{K}}_{j}|}{\max(K-|{\mathcal{K}}_{j}|,\,1)}.

D.3.1
(S1)
Single-mode change with equal-spaced change points

We consider when the change points are equally spaced with
\Theta=\big\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.75T\rfloor\big\}
under
(S1)
, see Tables
D.37
–
D.38
.

D.4
Results for mode-wise loading space estimation

For the implementation of mode-wise loading estimation, we focus on the single change scenario
(S3)
in which a rank-deficient transformation matrix
A_{2,2}
is applied to the first mode with
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}=\{1\}
.
Consequently, we have
\mathrm{col}(\Lambda_{1,k})=\mathrm{col}(\Lambda_{2,k})=\mathrm{col}(\Lambda_{k})
for
k\in\{2,3\}
.
Upon consistently detecting and locating
\theta_{1}=\lfloor T/2\rfloor
by TFMseg and identifying
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
as described in Section
3.2
, we compare two approaches to estimating
\mathrm{col}(\Lambda_{j,k}),\,j\in[2],\,k\in[3]
:

(M1)

A segment-wise estimator that applies the projection-based estimation method described in Section
3.1
, to each
(0,\widehat{\theta}_{1}]
and
(\widehat{\theta}_{1},T]
, separately.
This requires the estimation of
r_{j,k}
, the mode-
k
factor number on the
j
-th segment, which is achieved as described in Section
3.3
.

(M2)

A mode-informed estimator following Remark
3
, where
\Lambda_{1,1}
and
\Lambda_{2,1}
are estimated as in
(M1)
(denoted by
\widehat{\Lambda}_{1,1}
and
\widehat{\Lambda}_{2,1}
, respectively), while
\Lambda_{2}
is estimated as
\sqrt{p_{2}}
times the
\widehat{r}_{2}
leading eigenvectors of the matrix

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Tpp_{1}p_{2}}

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{\widehat{\theta}_{1}}\left(\textnormal{{mat}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{t})(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{3}\otimes\widehat{\Lambda}_{1,1})\right)\left(\textnormal{{mat}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{t})(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{3}\otimes\widehat{\Lambda}_{1,1})\right)^{\intercal}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\sum_{t=\widehat{\theta}_{1}+1}^{T}\left(\textnormal{{mat}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{t})(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{3}\otimes\widehat{\Lambda}_{2,1})\right)\left(\textnormal{{mat}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{t})(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{3}\otimes\widehat{\Lambda}_{2,1})\right)^{\intercal}\right\},

where
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{3}
is the preliminary estimator given in Section
3.1
; we estimate
\Lambda_{3}
analogously.

After applying TFMseg and the mode-identification procedure to
N=100
realizations, we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{D}}\subset[N]
the index set of realizations where the single change is detected (i.e.
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}
) and
\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}}_{1}={\mathcal{K}}_{1}
is returned.
Then for the realizations
n\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
, we measure the distance between the projection matrices onto the corresponding column spaces of the target matrix, say
A
, and its estimator
\widehat{A}
, as

\displaystyle d(\widehat{A},A)=\left\|\widehat{A}(\widehat{A}^{\intercal}\widehat{A})^{-1}\widehat{A}^{\intercal}-A(A^{\intercal}A)^{-1}A^{\intercal}\right\|,

(D.1)

see Figure
D.9
.
We truncate anomalously large estimation errors from the boxplots for the ease of comparing the results across different settings, noting that the large errors are solely attributed to that the factor number estimator occasionally under-estimates
r_{j,k}
over shorter segments post change point detection when
T
is small.

As expected, for modes 2 and 3, pooling the pre- and post-change point segments improves the estimation of the loading spaces, and the improvement is observable across all settings.
In line with Proposition
1
, we note that the mode
k
loading estimation error decreases with
p_{\text{-}k}
, which is noticeable when the dimensions are highly unbalanced.
For mode 1, the post-change estimation error is smaller as
r_{j,1}=\text{rank}(\Lambda_{j,1})
reduces from
3
to
2
after the change point.
This has an adverse effect on the other two modes, when observing the estimation errors form
(M1)
, which is accounted by that the projection involved in (
3.1
) post-change point is of reduced rank.

Figure D.9:
(S3)
Boxplots of mode-wise loading estimation errors with varying
T
(left to right) and
(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})
(top to bottom) over subset of realizations
n\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
. Segment pre (resp. Segment post) refers to segment-wise estimation errors using the data before (resp. after) the change point estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{1}
by
(M1)
.

D.5
Results for change point detection in the presence of missingness

To investigate the method described in Remark
2
for handling missing observations in the data, we introduce missingness to
\{\mathcal{X}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
generated under
(S1)
by setting an element
\mathcal{X}_{i_{1},\ldots,_{K},t}
to be missing if
t>\lfloor T/2\rfloor
and
i_{k}>\lfloor p_{k}/2\rfloor
for
k\in[K]
.
This is a structured missingness pattern explored in
16
.

Overall, we observe that the proposed modification to TFMseg handles missingness well; the detection of
\theta_{1}
is little influenced by the missingness, while detecting
\theta_{2}
and
\theta_{3}
becomes more challenging as the missingness is concentrated in the second half of the data. It suffers more from the missingness in the presence of serial correlations, which is considered to be more challenging even in the absence of missingness.

(S1)

\Theta=\big\{\lfloor 0.25T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.5T\rfloor,\lfloor 0.75T\rfloor\big\}

D.44
–
D.45
and Figures
D.10
–
D.11
; in the figures, we present the results obtained from the complete observations for comparison, which are taken from Appendix
D.2.2
.

Appendix E
Additional results for real data applications

For the Fama–French portfolio returns data analyzed in Section
6.2
,
Figures
E.1
–
E.2
plot the series of average absolute return
\{100^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{10}\sum_{j=1}^{10}|\mathcal{X}_{t,ij}|\}_{t=1}^{T}
, which shows that the market-wide fluctuation formed a sharp peak around the estimated break.

Average absolute returns from the value-weighted return series, with the change point estimator returned by TFMseg.

Figure E.2:
Average absolute returns from the equal-weighted return series, with the change point estimator returned by TFMseg.
